Abstract (英文): Capability benchmarks measure what large language models can do, but not whether their recommendations remain appropriate as decision conditions change. This paper proposes a trajectory-based measurement framework and prospective validation protocol for artificial wisdom, defined behaviorally as context-sensitive regulation of judgment. Versioned reference specifications determine admissible responses and transition materiality before evaluation. Seven stage-level criteria assess judgment under current conditions, while two transition components assess material-update adequacy and commitment-preserving stability. Stability requires prior adequacy; switching within registered commitment classes is permitted, and persistent error earns no stability credit. A nine-component profile is primary, with a secondary bottleneck score and separate critical-failure reporting. Three simplified simulations examine precision, bottleneck estimation, and sensitivity to model-family count. A single-criterion simulation met its precision criteria at 24 scenarios per domain. Near a bottleneck tie, the estimated minimum had a downward bias of 0.55 points at that size. A simplified incremental-prediction simulation detected a Brier improvement of 0.027 with probabilities of .508 and .742 using eight and twelve families, respectively; it does not establish power for the operational protocol. An exploratory predecessor-rubric study comprising 71 evaluation records illustrates aggregation sensitivity but does not validate the proposed instrument. The twelve-family target remains provisional pending feasibility assessment and calibration of the complete analysis. Whether the profile predicts independent decision outcomes beyond capability remains an open empirical question.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Efficient custom operators are essential for modern deep learning workloads, yet developing high-performance kernels for Ascend NPUs remains slow, expert-intensive, and difficult to automate. Although AI agents now approach expert performance on GPU kernel benchmarks, current models remain less capable of generating AscendC kernels for NPUs, potentially reflecting the relative scarcity of relevant training data. We thus present CAKE, a CANN-native system for deployable custom operators that combines adaptive multi-DSL routing, separate Generation and Evolution modes, and reviewed knowledge reuse through CAKE Book and a Self-Evolving Agent. On 53 CANN-Bench v1.1 operators on Ascend 950PR, CAKE's aggregated results achieve $100\%$ case pass rate on the standard set (1,060/1,060) and $98.28\%$ on the hidden set (4,167/4,240), with an overall mean score of 78.63 out of 100 and an average performance speedup of $1.334\times$ over the baseline. Evolution then pushes correct operators further: EngramGateFusion cuts aggregate device time by $46.6\%$ over 20 rounds and roughly halves the mean roofline gap. These results offer a practical path toward closing Ascend's AI-for-kernel capability gap.

Abstract (英文): Decision support has become sophisticated at exploring uncertainty, but much less so at representing the architecture of the decision itself. As a result, the decision space is often narrowed before uncertainty is analyzed, leaving interactions among choices and the effects of alternatives they imply largely invisible. This paper introduces multichoice modeling, a framework for making large decision configuration spaces explicit and computationally explorable under uncertainty. Drawing on the logic of rugged landscapes, we conceptualize such problems as contingent performance landscapes, where interdependent choices generate configuration-specific outcomes whose value changes across uncertain futures. The computational framework combines multidimensional decision and uncertainty spaces in a single simulation. Further, variance-based sensitivity analysis is used for identifying influential choices, and conditional distribution analysis for comparing configuration-specific performance profiles. We demonstrate the framework through three case studies: a manufacturing process upgrade, M&A strategy, and mortgage design. By contrasting conventional uncertainty-only analysis with multichoice modeling, the cases show how opening the decision configuration space can reveal unexpected complementarities, near-best configurations with different risk profiles, and strategies that were not apparent as alternatives at the outset.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Generative artificial intelligence promises to democratize personalized tutoring, yet the longitudinal stability of adaptive systems remains under-theorized. Current ethical discourse focuses on static bias. This paper argues that a distinct threat lies in algorithmic drift: a feedback loop in which minor, demographically asymmetric observation errors compound over time into systemic inequities. We make three contributions. First, we formalize algorithmic drift as a coupled learner--tutor dynamical system. Second, we corroborate this theory with an agent-based Monte Carlo simulation in which either a transparent online tracker or an LSTM deep-knowledge-tracing network drives an IRT-based curriculum policy. Third, we introduce the temporal fairness stabilizer (TFS), a targeted intervention that periodically re-probes learner capability, and benchmark it against standard exploration baselines with explicit group-fairness metrics and replication-level inference. Within the modeled environment, TFS closes the achievement gap where generic exploration does not, and we characterize the conditions under which it succeeds. We separate what is established analytically, what is demonstrated in simulation, and what remains to be validated with real learners. Our results reframe algorithmic fairness in education as a temporal property of a closed loop rather than a deployment-time checkbox.

</details>

Abstract (英文): While reasoning traces improve model capability, they also provide supervision signals for downstream distillation. Initial efforts to mitigate this issue rely on decoding-time interventions, but they incur high inference overhead and can harm the teacher model's natural fluency. We propose Conflict-Aware Logit Adapters (CALA), a lightweight training-time defense. CALA attaches a small residual adapter to a frozen teacher model and trains only the adapter. A proxy student identifies positions where imitation is most sensitive, and the adapter perturbs the output distribution at these positions while remaining within high-probability regions. A conflict-aware optimization strategy balances anti-distillation and utility objectives, while regularization preserves the teacher's performance and fluency. At inference time, CALA incurs modest overhead and requires no auxiliary model. Experiments on reasoning benchmarks show that CALA substantially reduces successful student imitation while maintaining near-original teacher performance and generation quality. These results establish CALA as a practical and efficient alternative to decoding-time approaches.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models (LLMs) have demonstrated strong potential to automate heuristic design for NP-hard combinatorial optimization (CO) tasks without human intervention. Recent methods predominantly rely on evolutionary computation (EC) frameworks, where heuristic diversification is mainly induced through EC-like operators or downstream selection, while the direction of heuristic transformation remains largely unharnessed. In this paper, we propose a multi-directional Perturbation-Rewrite Search (PRS) framework for automatic heuristic design that factorizes heuristic transformation through directional search primitives at the generation level. Specifically, PRS operates on heuristic ideas through three complementary directions: expansion toward richer and more expressive designs, compression toward simpler and less specialized designs, and steering for fine-grained refinement, together enabling broader and more sustained exploration of diverse regions in the heuristic design space. PRS further organizes candidate exploration within a breadth-first search-based process with adaptive operator control, which ensures thorough candidate utilization and sustained exploration, and maintains strong search dynamics while preventing premature convergence. Extensive empirical results across diverse CO tasks show that PRS consistently discovers high-quality heuristics, demonstrating the effectiveness and generality of explicitly directional exploration design.

</details>

Abstract (英文): As large language models are deployed in high-stakes roles such as therapy, education, and personal assistance, their social cognition must be assessed reliably. Current Theory of Mind (ToM) benchmarks report aggregate scores that conflate two distinct competencies: explicit ToM (recognizing what someone believes) and applied ToM (using that belief to predict behavior). Building on this distinction, we investigate the gap and its recoverability using four structurally diverse benchmarks. Aggregate reporting obscures uneven competence: on SimpleToM, Llama-3.1-8B scores 51.5 percent overall while recognizing mental states at 99.4 percent and predicting behavior at only 28.9 percent. To assess recovery, we compare structured prompting, matched in-context learning, and our proposed Belief-State Test-Time Training (BSTTT), which adapts lightweight parameters on answered support questions through action reconstruction. Across Qwen2.5-7B, Llama-3.1-8B, and GPT-oss-20B, recovery depends on the model, task, and intervention. On SimpleToM, reminders combined with BSTTT improve Llama's behavior prediction by 40.8 percentage points; reminders account for most of the overall improvement, with BSTTT adding 1.3 to 5.5 points beyond reminders across models. Contextual examples often outperform weight updates, while BSTTT remains stronger in specific model-benchmark settings. Temporal belief tracking remains difficult despite partial recovery. These findings show that aggregate ToM scores can overstate applied competence while hiding performance recoverable through targeted assistance. We recommend decomposed reporting of explicit and applied accuracy alongside intervention comparisons that establish which failures are recoverable and under what conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Policy enforcement for LLM agents has shifted from single-turn content moderation to compound, application-specific policies evaluated over multi-turn transcripts and tool-call trajectories. Fine-tuned guardrail models are efficient but degrade on compound policies, and while LLM-as-a-judge approaches achieve higher accuracy, they incur high inference cost and tend to produce unstable predictions. Neurosymbolic methods offer a natural alternative, decomposing the policy into narrow neural decisions composed under explicit logic. However, existing symbolic layers lack the required expressiveness and are optimized against fixed data rather than the policy itself. To address this, we introduce SCOPE, which compiles a policy into an executable program of neural predicates executed by small models, deterministic extractors, and code that composes their outputs. The program is optimized jointly with its training data, using adversarial generation of counterexamples that target where the program deviates from the policy. We evaluate SCOPE on a benchmark of real-world policy tasks against prompt optimization, task decomposition, and workflow generation methods, achieving the highest average accuracy across tasks and models. Programs optimized with the weakest executor transfer to other models without further optimization. This allows them to be deployed in production on inexpensive models, where they reach accuracy comparable to frontier reasoning models. Unlike these monolithic models, SCOPE produces substantially more stable predictions, and each verdict is interpretable through the predicate outputs that produced it. Additionally, we validate the key components of our method through an ablation study. SCOPE thus enables reliable, interpretable, and efficient policy enforcement at production scale.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World Models (WMs) can support Multimodal Agents (MAs) through simulated experience, while Agent trajectories can inform World Model refinement. Evaluating this interaction requires separate evidence about action selection and state evolution. We introduce Dual Exam, which tests Agents under fixed environment response mechanisms and World Models under fixed action interventions across physical, digital, social, and scientific domains. The Agent track compares 10 configurations on 1,088 items in 15 reporting categories. The World Model track contains 400 cases across eight scenarios; 15 configurations have full coverage and one covers six scenarios. GPT 6 Astra leads the case-weighted Agent Index at 31.3, and GPT-Image-2 leads the full-coverage WM Overall at 21.7. These scores use different tasks and metrics and are not directly comparable. A 150-case pilot of one coupled pairing records zero task successes and motivates component-level diagnosis. Results reveal uneven task performance and failures to preserve task-relevant state. Their value for predicting coupled success or sustained reciprocal learning remains to be established.

Abstract (英文): Artificial intelligence has advanced through scale, data, and computation, yet remains bounded by its representational framework. We ask what makes intelligence open-ended. Every inferential system selects meaningful distinctions, limiting what it can learn. Within a fixed purpose, performance reaches a ceiling set by its semantic domain. Teleological succession, in which purposes arise from earlier ones, faces a similar limit: Boolean-closed purpose-transformations exhaust their reachable semantic territory. We shift from purposes to inferential systems. Drawing on Kant and Gödel, we model cognitive states as partial evaluations over a formal language. Unresolved propositions may be undecided or undecidable. Reasoning can resolve the former but not the latter, leaving a frontier for extension. Non-trivial reductions of undecidable propositions expose undecidable intermediate premises whose reflective reification supports multiple decisive extensions. Finite trace-guided generation remains fallible, requiring meta-level selection and polycontextural exploration. Using Kolmogorov complexity, we show that no architecture with a fixed state description, transition mechanism, and initial condition can generate histories incorporating irreducible algorithmic information. Because physical systems are fixed in this sense, we examine collectives. Coupling finitely many finite agents does not remove the bound. Extension requires persistent external memory preserving semantic content and historical coordinates. Collective generative capacity depends on balancing overlap for transfer with difference for generation. We propose semantic self-extension as a design principle: intelligence should not merely resolve ambiguity within existing meanings, but transform unresolved structures into new spaces of meaning, generating new distinctions, questions, representations, and purposes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We present an optimized approach for finding minimal move sequences to solve Flood-It puzzles using A* search and compare it with approaches using SAT, Integer Programming, and Constraint Programming solvers. The performance of A* search is considerably improved by using an appropriate heuristic and sophisticated equivalences of game states to limit the search space. Each aspect is systematically evaluated, providing insights that generalize to other problems. By comparing the best variant of each approach, the A* search is the fastest in finding a minimal solution in about 75 % of the Flood-It instances tested. This result is in line with our expectation since A* specifically looks for a minimum solution. In contrast, the SAT-based approach is designed to solve the decision problem of whether a solution with a given length exists. An iterative search is required to determine a minimum solution length using a SAT-based approach. However, with increasing complexity, quantified by the minimum solution length, the SAT-based approach outperforms A*. We also compared the different approaches to the decision problem, where A* again performed best for the largest number of puzzles. Here, again, SAT supersedes A* for higher complexities, albeit with a lower crossing point.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We present DeepOpen, a decision-Oriented intent-classification framework with dataset- specific systems for BANKING77 and CLINC150. On the 150 in-scope CLINC150 classes, probability fusion of one Laya specialist and three DeBERTa-v3-large classifiers achieves 98.0222% (4,411/4,500). Thus, the continuation does not establish a reliable accuracy gain. Abstract (英文): Multi-agent LLM systems are increasingly used in practice, but their behavior is complex and hard to understand. We design a simple task to ask which model has the most influence on the final consensus when several models are mixed in one system. Experimentally, across seven model families, we find that the opinions of the other models are absorbed into the opinion of the stronger model, the one with more parameters. This happens even though the task requires no pretraining knowledge and has no correct answer. To understand this phenomenon, we interpret the agents as weighted particles moving on a simplex. Larger models carry a larger weight and therefore occupy a deeper well in the energy landscape. A large weight means strong in-context learning: a model with a large weight amplifies the majority of its context. Larger models are generally better at in-context learning, and this is what makes the larger model the influencer. We hope that this work helps to interpret multi-agent systems, whose behavior is becoming too complex for humans to understand.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in large language models and agentic workflows offer a new paradigm to early-stage structural design translating design intent into structural models. Although text-to-3D approaches have demonstrated that natural language can be used to produce visual, mesh-based geometric models, structural design requires domain-specific geometric arrangements and semantically rich data types. This paper introduces a proof-of-concept workflow for prompt-to-structure generation using an AI agent connected to a COMPAS-based structural modelling API through a Model Context Protocol (MCP) server. Through controlled modelling operations, the agent creates and modifies a graph-based structural data model composed of explicit building elements. The resulting artifacts can be inspected, modified, serialised, and further connected to downstream AEC workflows. The approach is demonstrated through selected prompt-to-structure examples and by progressive and editable design sequences that resemble typical iterative structural design workflows. The results are preliminary, but demonstrate the possibility of combining natural language, agentic workflows, and domain-specific structural data structures as a first step toward AI-assisted structural co-design.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We focus on optimisation-based Decision Support Systems in real-world applications. In this context, we aim to provide contrastive explanations that answer users' questions about recommendations based on the solution of an optimisation problem, specifically a Mixed-Integer Program (MIP). Addressing this endeavour is crucial to ensure human oversight of the decisions implemented, as required by international guidelines on trustworthy and responsible automated decision-making systems. We propose a novel methodology that combines constraint reasoning techniques and Large Language Models (LLMs) to generate contrastive explanations for optimisation problems encoded as MIPs. Although LLMs have shown impressive capabilities in Natural Language processing, they can produce incorrect answers due to reasoning limitations. To overcome these limitations, we leverage constraint reasoning techniques to distil the information necessary to produce an explanation, which is then passed as input to an LLM as the so-called graph of reasons. Such a graph captures the semantics and the relationships among constraints of the optimisation problem. Results on real-world scheduling domains show that incorporating constraint reasoning improves explanations generated by the LLM. In particular, using the graph of reasons enables the LLM to produce natural-language explanations that include more relevant reasons while reducing errors compared to explanations generated without this structure.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion language models generate multiple token positions in parallel, but few-step decoding makes parallel commitment difficult: tokens that appear reliable individually may be jointly inconsistent, while premature commitments can prevent later context-dependent refinement. In this paper, we introduce SetCommit, a training-free method for coordinated parallel token commitment. Instead of selecting tokens independently by confidence, SetCommit selects a commitment set that balances token reliability against local joint commitment risk, preserving conditioning opportunities for subsequent denoising steps. It further performs budget-neutral local exchanges, replacing vulnerable earlier commitments when newly revealed context supports more reliable alternatives. SetCommit requires no retraining or architectural modification and is compatible with existing diffusion decoding strategies. Experiments with LLaDA-8B and Dream-7B on GSM8K, MATH-500, MBPP, and HumanEval show accuracy/pass@1 gains of up to 7.4 percentage points over native decoding at 32 model evaluations. At common quality targets, geometric-mean incremental speedups range from $1.44$ to $1.74\times$. Code is available at https://github.com/Kissmetothemoon/SetCommit.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Offline tests can fail to reveal whether an agent update will remain safe after deployment. We operationalize this post-audit mirage using matched safe and harmful lifecycles that expose identical verifier-visible evidence while an isolated scorer records opposite deployment truth. Our theorem applies standard observational-equivalence and indistinguishability ideas to this executable audit boundary and states the resulting error-abstention tradeoff. The Post-Audit Mirage benchmark makes that boundary executable across authorization, scheduling, and shared-capacity environments. IdentifiedRangeMonitor uses fresh group-labelled harm outcomes to return deploy, hold, cannot determine, or unsupported. Experiments show that monitoring can recover the missing distinction in favorable settings but may remain undecided for rare groups, small effects, or missing observations. Verifier-agent evaluation must therefore test both calculation validity and whether the available evidence supports the deployment decision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diffusion Language Models (DLMs) introduce unique quantization challenges due to their iterative denoising process. In this work, we reveal that quantization sensitivity is both taskand time-dependent, with different downstream tasks exhibiting distinct precision requirements across denoising steps. We further show that these patterns can be predicted without groundtruth labels or task-specific evaluation metrics. Building on these findings, we introduce T 2Quant, a task and time-aware mixedprecision quantization framework that uses a label-free sensitivity signal to optimally allocate activation precision across denoising steps under a fixed bit-width budget. Extensive experiments across three DLMs and six benchmarks demonstrate that T 2Quant consistently improves the average accuracy of existing quantization methods, achieving gains of up to 6.7 percentage points under the same budget.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We envision a general-purpose protein foundation model as a unified, interactive model of sequence, structure, and function, capable of understanding, generation, and cross-modal translation while retaining the instruction-following and reasoning capabilities of modern LLMs. Toward this vision, we introduce Trion-1, the first native, unified, multimodal diffusion language model (dLLM) for proteins. Trion-1 is built on two key design choices: (1) a unified discrete token space that jointly represents sequence, structure, and natural language; (2) a dLLM backbone that replaces strict left-to-right factorization with bidirectional or semi-bidirectional token interactions, enabling global residue communication and long-range contact modeling that are naturally suited to structure modeling. This formulation unifies six core capabilities: folding, inverse folding, sequence understanding, structure understanding, sequence design, and structure design, while retaining the native language capabilities of general-purpose LLMs. On established benchmarks, Trion-1 achieves broadly competitive performance. In folding, Trion-1 achieves a TM-score above 0.8 with a single sample under strict homology control, matching or exceeding the existing tri-modal model using 200 samples. For sequence understanding, Trion-1 performs on par with top-tier LLMs such as Claude Opus 4.7 (62.37%). These results support well-aligned sequence-structure-language representations and strong cross-modal generative capabilities.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reinforcement learning has emerged as an effective approach for combinatorial optimization, but most existing methods focus on problems such as the Travelling Salesperson Problem (TSP) in which all elements must be served. Selective combinatorial optimization problems (SCOPs), including the Orienteering Problem (OP), Prize-Collecting TSP (PCTSP), Stochastic PCTSP and Knapsack Problem (KP), additionally require selecting which elements to include. This selection creates sampled solutions with substantially different objective values, yet standard preference optimization reduces such comparisons to binary rankings and discards the magnitude of these differences.
  We introduce Reward-Weighted Preference Optimization (RWPO), a pairwise training objective that incorporates reward-gap information into preference learning. For candidate trajectories sampled from the same instance, RWPO weights each preference loss according to the normalized difference in their rewards, assigning greater training emphasis to comparisons that represent larger improvements in solution quality. We further combine RWPO with scale-specific fine-tuning and reward- and likelihood-weighted tilted sampling to adapt trained policies to larger and shifted instance distributions without relying on local-search post-processing.
  Experiments on the OP, PCTSP, Stochastic PCTSP, and KP show that RWPO consistently improves over unweighted preference optimization and policy-gradient baselines across a wide range of instance sizes. On the OP, the resulting solver exceeds the mean reward of the Compass metaheuristic at scales from 100 to 2,000 nodes. The results demonstrate that preserving reward-gap information can substantially improve preference-based learning for selective combinatorial optimization and support generalization to large-scale instances.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A strong direct tree model may remain the best standalone photovoltaic forecaster, yet a weighted combination can reduce its residual errors. We examine whether complementary temporal predictors can lower the tree's mean absolute error (MAE) over 15--240 minutes without weaker ensemble members degrading performance. The candidate bank contains LightGBM and seven neural implementations with different temporal representations. A combiner fitted to MAE has nonnegative weights that sum to one. Unhelpful candidates can receive zero weight, while the tree anchors the combination and provides a development-stage fallback. The main design shares weights across horizons; horizon-specific weights and alternative losses test whether further complexity is needed. On 56,078 windows from two co-located public PVDAQ systems, the historical shared-MAE policy reduces scaled MAE by 3.21\% relative to the tree.
  The latest bounded neural correction does not reduce MAE further than this static combination. The results support selective combination of complementary predictions in this bank rather than equal averaging or indiscriminate addition of modules. Both analyses reuse a previously inspected evaluation period. They do not establish independent replication, weather-specific causation or universal gains from eight experts.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multimodal Large Language Models have shown promising performance in computer-use tasks, from planning user workflows to grounding natural-language actions to precise UI targets. Existing end-to-end benchmarks primarily emphasize long-horizon task completion, while GUI grounding benchmarks remain focused on conventional controls and a limited set of professional interfaces. We introduce POINTERBENCH, a diagnostic suite of 1,500 screenshot-instruction pairs probing three complementary capabilities: thin sub-element identification and geometric grounding in spreadsheets (POINTERBENCH-Sheets), character-level grounding in long-text documents (POINTERBENCH-Text), and mixed GUI grounding across 100 professional applications (POINTERBENCH-Pro). We carefully curate each task with pixel-exact target geometry and evaluate both general-purpose multimodal models and 39 specialist GUI grounding configurations. We find text grounding to be the most challenging setting, with Claude Fable 5, GPT-5.6 Sol, and the leading native specialist UI-Venus-2-9B achieving 69.6%, 53.4%, and 25.8% success on POINTERBENCH-Text despite respective 83.2%, 77.7%, and 54.5% average success across POINTERBENCH. These results reveal substantial headroom in fine-grained GUI grounding across frontier and specialist models. We publicly release the benchmark and evaluation code at https://github.com/warmwindOS/pointerbench.

Abstract (英文): Long-horizon coding agents can observe a failure and generate a plausible critique yet continue along the same incorrect trajectory. We call this the Critique-to-Action Handoff Gap. We introduce RIPPLE, a recovery-oriented training framework that internalizes process-level guidance into a single self-recovering policy. A prefix-only teacher provides evidence-grounded recovery supervision without access to future actions or terminal outcomes. After lightweight distillation, reinforcement learning assigns stage-specific credit to reflection triggering, diagnosis, and corrective action. Local validity signals gate trajectory advantages so that intermediate recovery decisions do not indiscriminately inherit terminal outcomes. Across four repository-level software-engineering benchmarks, RIPPLE improves the base policy by an arithmetic mean of 4.8 score points, with no auxiliary critic at deployment. Controlled ablations isolate the benefits of local, stage-aligned credit, and matched-prefix interventions show that critique semantics affect subsequent correction. Recovery gains concentrate where the prefix contains sufficient diagnostic evidence and the repository state remains reversible within the remaining budget.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Memory enables language agents to carry useful distinctions from past experience into future decisions. Its value depends on which distinctions survive, whether a bounded agent can use them, and what can be recovered or revised as demands evolve. We develop a decision-theoretic framework that follows this value from raw history through persistent state, operational use, and costly recovery and revision. Original and retained value are expressed in units of avoided task loss; risk-cost frontiers describe attainable recovery and preservation across uses. Unknown demand yields value profiles, while interactive feedback changes the distribution of future work. A cost-aware simulation condition and five minimal counterexamples expose the roles of information timing, retrieval access, component interaction, feedback and demand uncertainty. In particular, a pointer with zero mutual information about the answer can still reduce the cost of exact recovery. Diagnostics on LoCoMo, a two-project update task and longitudinal cases exhibit failures of preservation, use and measurement. At fixed retrieved evidence, a saved ledger changes subsequent answers; at fixed agent outputs, an evaluator revision changes scores. The framework connects these distinctions to operational risk bounds and testable principles for allocating memory, preserving recovery options and revising evidence dependencies.

</details>

Abstract (英文): GUI agents aim to automate complex user tasks by interacting with mobile interfaces, but ef- fective decision-making in long-horizon set- tings requires reusing prior experience rather than relying solely on the environment ob- servations. Existing memory-based methods typically store experience either at the single- action level or as entire trajectories. Single- step actions are often too fine-grained to carry meaningful semantics, since the success of an individual action does not necessarily imply progress towards the overall goal. In contrast, whole trajectories are often too coarse-grained and difficult to reuse across different tasks. We propose a subgoal-aware memory graph that distils past trajectories into structured, reusable evidence at the level of subgoals. We define a subgoal as consecutive actions shar- ing a single intent, the granularity at which progress toward the task becomes meaning- ful. To reduce redundancy and support cross- task reuse, we organise subgoal experiences as nodes in a graph, where related memories can be merged, extended, and retrieved based on the environment observation. On SPA-Bench, our method improves success rate by 11.11% over the baseline.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tree-based speculative decoding accelerates large language model inference by verifying multiple candidate branches in parallel. However, in practical serving scenarios, similar requests repeatedly induce overlapping candidate branches. While retrieval-based methods mitigate this by reusing historical drafts, the resulting candidate trees often contain many low-quality branches as the candidate tree grows, which reduces verification efficiency and wastes target-side computation. To address this, we propose ValuSpec, a plug-and-play candidate valuation mechanism that filters low-quality branches from a supplied candidate tree before expensive target-side verification. ValuSpec introduces transfer-based supervision to align filtering with the target model's behavior. A filtering threshold is estimated from the target model's greedy trajectories on a calibration split, without any parameter training. The target model then verifies the filtered tree under the output-identical greedy rule, so accepted tokens are guaranteed to match standard autoregressive decoding exactly. Experimental results on HumanEval, GSM8K, Dolly, and SpecBench show that, in our main evaluation, ValuSpec achieves up to 2.38x end-to-end speedup in retrieval-based tree settings. Furthermore, integrating ValuSpec into the EAGLE-3 dynamic tree framework yields up to 2.78x end-to-end speedup without modifying its tree construction strategy, demonstrating that ValuSpec serves as a generally applicable and plug-and-play candidate valuation module.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The rise of Agentic AI systems has changed the landscape of artificial intelligence by enabling strong reasoning capabilities through specialized multi-agent interaction with structured self-reflection. However, in target-driven reasoning tasks, single- and multi-agent systems are often on par in terms of performance gains. A major challenge is that failures often arise from an ineffective exchange of critical information during inter-agent interaction and coordination. Different from existing multi-agent debate paradigms that rely on synchronous turn-taking and sharing information across different agents, we propose an inter-agent protocol based on shared Memory bUFFer INference - referred to as MUFFIN - in an asynchronous agentic framework. In the first step, an orchestrator routes queries based on their reasoning demands and delegates demanding ones to a multi-agent setting, where a shared memory buffer provides asynchronous exchange of intermediate findings while an agent's private reasoning remains local. Our experiments show that our framework Muffin outperforms established competitors, achieving the best accuracy in 17 out of 20 of our benchmark settings, and provides a better quality-cost trade-off than multi-agent debate: Muffin improves accuracy by 19.2 accuracy points while using 77.4% fewer tokens, without requiring full-trace sharing.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Autonomous research agents are increasingly used to automate large portions of scientific research, yet they are prone to research integrity violations (RIVs): behaviors that compromise the validity, reproducibility, or faithfulness of the research process. We present RIDAR, a framework for automated RIV detection and runtime intervention. First, we introduce RIVT, a comprehensive taxonomy of 14 RIV types spanning the full research lifecycle. Building on RIVT, we present RIDAR-post-hoc, a framework to detect RIVs within agent trajectories. RIDAR-post-hoc achieves 85.2% F1 performance on RIV-Gold, a benchmark for RIV detection with 60 expert-annotated trajectories. Applying RIDAR-post-hoc to RIV-Wild, a dataset of 300 agent trajectories across 50 distinct research tasks, we detect 3,964 RIV instances in 99.7% of trajectories, revealing the prevalence of RIVs in current agents. Finally, we present RIDAR-runtime, a framework for dynamically detecting and correcting RIVs during agent runtime. RIDAR-runtime effectively reduces agent RIVs remaining at task completion by 36.8%. We will release our code and data to facilitate future research.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This paper studies the problem of safe tool execution for large language model (LLM) agents under prompt injection. Using Petri nets, we formalize the execution harness between the LLM and its tools as a supervisory control problem, in which marks represent capabilities, approvals and budgets. Since a transition fires only if the marks it needs are present, an injected request whose preconditions are unmet or already spent is refused regardless of its wording. Treating tool outcomes as uncontrollable, we enforce safety by the conventional maximally permissive supervisor, and this safety transfers to the running system under four assumptions. Moreover, to capture an agent that may propose again after a refusal, we introduce the notion of a replanning closed loop, in which each retry costs an inference call. We show that, in this loop, the conventional supervisor can be arbitrarily more intrusive than a stricter safe one. Furthermore, for a given proposer model, the maximally permissive least-intrusive supervisor is characterized as a value-threshold refinement of the conventional one. Under precise conditions, this refinement orders two concurrent actions by at most one monitor place. On an autonomous release agent, we find by synthesis that safe deployment requires a step that the supervisor can refuse after a rollout starts.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World action models increasingly rely on pretrained video models assumed to capture physical dynamics for prediction, planning, and action generation. Existing evaluations assess plausibility, probe representations, or measure predictive surprise, but rarely identify which history-conditioned physical expectations conflict with observations. We introduce PhysTrace, a training-free framework aligning MLLM-generated, history-conditioned expectations with observed video windows to temporally localize physical violations and provide interpretable descriptions without task-specific probes or classifiers. We also introduce IntPhys2-Timing, adding violation intervals to IntPhys2. PhysTrace achieves a Hit Rate of up to 70.7% across physical principles on IntPhys2-Timing and outperforms the compared methods in overall possible-impossible discrimination accuracy on IntPhys2. Applied to reconstructed videos along the trajectories of pretrained diffusion/flow-based video models, PhysTrace reveals attribute- and noise-dependent patterns in physical violation localization, enabling interpretable physical consistency analysis across timesteps.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Automated agentic-system design is typically evaluated on static task suites with immediate scalar rewards. Deployed systems face persistent shift, delayed dependent feedback, correlated component failures, and costly architecture changes. We formalize meta-agent architecture search as sequential incumbent replacement with switching costs and hard risk constraints and introduce Conservative Meta-Agent Search (CMAS). CMAS retrieves archived architectures and proposes typed graph edits, then scores each against the incumbent by paired shadow replay under common random numbers, with interventional component credit. Promotion requires an empirically calibrated, variance-penalized simultaneous score above a pre-specified margin, and is followed by a simulated canary stage and pre-specified rollback. Since backtests cannot certify meta-level skill, we introduce MetaInvest-Bench, a regime-switching simulator whose specified worker competence makes a surrogate-optimal architecture computable, with leakage-controlled replay, adversarial governance tracks, and five negative controls. Financial streams serve as a risk-sensitive testbed for meta-level generalization; we claim no alpha. Across 96 streams, CMAS cuts normalized mean surrogate dynamic-oracle regret from 0.116 to 0.104 and raises held-out utility from 0.44 to 0.48 over the strongest adaptive baseline; when extra workers add no value, it contracts to near-single-agent teams. Its deployment inference cost is ~6% lower, though its search-inclusive token cost is the highest of the methods compared. The observed per-update false-promotion rate is 3.4% against a nominal 5% target, and 4.8% in a sealed rerun. The calibration is empirical: it concerns an update-level replay statistic on an author-designed benchmark, and guarantees neither full-utility improvement, nor absence of simulated canary-stage harm, nor sequence-level error control.

</details>

Abstract (英文): When a user says "Keep it short", how short should the LLM's response be? More generally, users and applications require different levels of instruction adherence, but prompting does not reliably provide fine-grained control over them. We therefore study "steerable language models" that provide fine-grained numerical control over the degree to which models follow general instructions specified in text. However, existing approaches do not jointly offer fine-grained control, efficiency, and scalability across diverse instructions. To address this gap, we introduce DIAL (Distilled Instruction-Adherence Levels) to achieve all three properties. For fine-grained control, we perform classifier-free guidance (CFG) with a frozen LLM to provide training signals for on-policy distillation of instruction adherence at different levels. For efficiency, we design a lightweight steering adapter that uses a classifier over the LLM's hidden states to compute a closed-form steering edit. The classifier adaptively sets the steering magnitude at each token based on the target adherence level, while accounting for the geometry of the LLM's hidden states to preserve response coherence. For scalability, the classifier is text-conditioned, allowing a shared adapter to support new instructions without per-instruction fitting, and training requires only instruction and request text. Evaluation across multiple LLM families shows that DIAL achieves a competitive balance between concept expression and response fluency, with concept scores increasing monotonically across adherence levels. We introduce SteerIF, an instruction-only dataset of 98, 625 concept, persona, verifiable, and descriptive instructions. When trained on SteerIF, DIAL provides fine-grained control over personas and, when combined with prompting, improves verifiable instruction following.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reusable skills allow large language model agents to package task-specific procedures, tool affordances, and execution guidance into modular units. Prior work has largely studied how agents retrieve, compose, or synthesize skills before execution. In coding agents, however, execution poses an additional runtime control problem: the same task may be solved through primitive tool calls, macro skills, or hybrid execution that starts with a macro and falls back to micro-level recovery. We introduce Adaptive Skill Granularity, a framework that treats skill granularity as an action-abstraction policy conditioned on task structure, execution state, and macro-skill failures. The system combines a feature-based granularity controller and a finite-state recovery executor. We evaluate the framework with a deterministic coding-agent protocol using verifier-defined success, total cost, and process metrics such as macro adoption, failure recovery, route match, and transition correctness. Results show that fixed macro execution is efficient when applicable but brittle under mismatch, while runtime routing and explicit macro-to-micro recovery improve robustness and recover controlled macro failures that fixed-granularity execution leaves unresolved. Our findings suggest that skill libraries for coding agents should be treated not only as retrievable resources, but also as controllable, verifiable, and recoverable execution abstractions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recommender systems typically serve one user at a time. However, in households, members share a finite pantry and follow different dietary rules, making independent recommendations prone to resource conflicts and dietary violations. We formulate household food recommendation as constrained fair division and present FairFRESH, a neuro-symbolic framework that learns per-member utilities, allocates foods under explicit welfare objectives, enforces dietary rules through a deterministic filter, and explains recommendations using Shapley and Banzhaf attributions. Theoretically, we show that no finite penalty guarantees compliance near a dietary-rule threshold, establish approximation guarantees for greedy allocation under utilitarian and smoothed Nash objectives in the stock-free model before filtering, and characterize the scale invariance of Nash welfare. We also use the minimax theorem to analyze rotating shared meals. On NHANES-derived data, the learned utility model achieves Recall@20 of 0.293, outperforming five learned recommenders (at most 0.244) but not a previous-day repetition heuristic (0.321). On held-out four-person households, Nash allocation reduces Gini disparity by 42% relative to conflict resolution while improving Recall@20 from 0.180 to 0.197. It comes within 2% of an upper bound on the smoothed Nash optimum, and rescaling one member's utilities changes at most 1.5% of assignments. All recommendations satisfy the encoded dietary rules, while attribution scores correlate with utility loss upon component removal (r = 0.76). In simulation, shared meals reduce pantry use by about one-third, and weekly rotation increases the worst-off member's utility by 38% over the best fixed meal.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Coordinating two players in a cooking task requires a vision-language agent to track changing scene states, sequence recipe steps, and allocate shared resources under a time budget. We introduce an Overcooked-inspired benchmark for evaluating these capabilities in Unreal Engine environments constructed using coding agents. A single vision-language model controls both players from top-down visual observations, selecting high-level actions while the engine executes navigation. The benchmark contains 20 maps in five categories. These categories support evaluation of centralized coordination under equipment contention, changing terrain, and handoff dependencies. A prop legend and pot ingredient-count indicators provide explicit visual cues, while grid-based destinations support spatial grounding. The evaluation compares four VLMs on all 20 maps, measuring task score, order completion, delivery precision, action acceptance rate, and model API expenditure. A memory ablation on the same 20 maps examines the effect of a shared, self-authored notebook while retaining six-response action history in both conditions. The strongest evaluated model, \texttt{gpt-6-astra}, achieves 26.2\% mean order completion. Notebook memory has mixed score effects across models, with positive mean differences for Claude and Qwen and negative differences for GPT and Gemini.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to generate domain-specific commercial & medical content (e.g., claims, statements, and promotional phrases) in regulated industries such as pharmaceuticals. However, ensuring its regulatory compliance, factual accuracy, and audience-specific personalization remains a major challenge. We report a production case study asking whether moving compliance and personalization signal from prompt-level rules into retrieval time structured context improves pharmaceutical claims generation, using Composer, a deployed internal tool built on a Golden Data Model (GDM) of 1,867 pharmaceutical claims spanning four products. The GDM integrates claim metadata, references, audience labels, material type, usage history, Match Text Variant (MTV) links, and a six dimension quality score (6C). Across six product-audience segments, the context pipeline improves intended indication (+29%), intended content type (+62%) and intended target audience (+67%), 6C quality score (+86%), and claim reuse (+34%). Production log analysis over the deployment period shows a 29.5% reduction in weekly API cost. We report this as descriptive deployment evidence. This work is a systems and data architecture contribution: it shows that in a regulated, high-stakes domain, restructuring claim data for retrieval-time filtering is a more scalable lever than expanding prompt-level rules, and documents the engineering path data cleaning, graph linking, and quality scoring.

Abstract (英文): Individual preferences often become visible only after deployment, when a user accepts one decision or revokes it in favor of another. Jev-style typed interfaces make this feedback explicit by identifying the selected action and, under revocation, the decision it replaces. We study how these consequences persist across requests as Decision-Level Continual Learning from Typed Feedback (Jev-DLC). Jev-DLC treats each correction as both evidence about a recurring action identity and a directed relation between the preferred and rejected choices. It preserves these signals in complementary bounded channels above a frozen scorer and combines their scores to shape the next decision. The method retains no raw interaction history, adds no scorer call, and performs no runtime language-model backward pass. We also introduce TCPA-Bench, a chronological suite that credits a correction only through its effect on later decisions. On TCPA-Repeat, Jev-DLC reaches 44.83% user-macro accuracy overall, 2.43 points above Recent-4 history replay. After at least two earlier feedback events, it reaches 56.07%, a 7.54-point margin over the strongest reported comparison at this depth. These results show how explicit typed corrections can become compact causal state for continual personalization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hidden future-token leakage can create false architectural gains: an autoregressive model may appear substantially better under teacher-forced evaluation while its free-running generation deteriorates. We investigate this failure in an adaptive residual-routing decoder whose sequence-level routing summary exposes prefix positions to future tokens despite a correct causal attention mask. An anomalous 24-layer checkpoint reduces validation loss from the baseline's 3.5958 to 1.4259, yet it fails a prefix-invariance audit and has substantially higher reference-model perplexity during generation. Layer-wise diagnostics and implementation inspection trace the violation to a full-sequence summary broadcast through the dynamic routing-query path. Two independently trained 12-layer causal repairs eliminate detectable leakage and return validation loss and reference perplexity to the control scale, removing the large apparent advantage. As diagnostic tools, we use the established prefix-invariance contract, maximum prefix-output discrepancy (LeakScore), complementary behavioral metrics, and layer-wise profiles. Supporting experiments cover six synthetic implementation pairs spanning five module families, clean pretrained controls, controlled fault injection, and cache consistency across numerical precisions. These results illustrate how hidden future-token access can produce misleading claims of architectural progress and why lower teacher-forced loss should be interpreted together with whole-model causal checks and free-running evaluation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Industrial organizations hold operational knowledge in structured records, such as telemetry, point lists, and transactions, and in unstructured sources, such as manuals, service notes, communications, and inspection reports. Governance programs often manage these sources separately. Classifying, linking, and documenting them depends on a small group of employees with deep domain knowledge. Agents built on large language models can propose metadata, cite documents, and flag quality issues, but unreviewed proposals can spread errors into downstream systems. A perfunctory approval leaves no record of what a reviewer checked. We present contestable governance: specialist agents produce scored, cited arguments for disputed metadata decisions; a steward can accept, reject, modify, or add arguments before commit; and a governance record retains the evidence, interventions, and outcome. A versioned domain glossary supplies model-specific terms and acronym definitions to the agents. We describe the workflow, a triage path that reserves full argumentation for uncertain proposals while preserving review before commit, the scoring and record models, and a gate that controls which verified decisions become precedent. Two HVAC use cases illustrate the pattern: point classification using Brick Schema class labels, supported by an open reference implementation, and an illustrative warranty failure-mode decision linking claim fields and telemetry to service notes and revisioned bulletins.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Masked diffusion language models generate tokens in parallel, but their efficiency depends on which predictions are committed. Learned unmasking policies improve the speed--accuracy trade-off, yet differ in supervision, inputs, and capacity, obscuring which choices matter. We view supervised policies as distilling a decoding procedure through a teacher and examine independently what the teacher labels as committable, on which states the policy imitates it, what the policy observes, and how much the number of function evaluations (NFE) must decrease to offset policy overhead. We find that labeling predictions matching the final output is overly permissive at low NFE, and hidden states are weak alone but can complement confidence. About a 10% NFE reduction suffices to offset policy overhead for policies up to 7.5M parameters across tested settings. We therefore train a policy on trajectory-contraction targets, which accept only commitments staying on a reference trajectory, using confidence and hidden states. On LLaDA-8B-Instruct, the resulting 856K-parameter policy achieves the best accuracy--NFE frontier and highest throughput on GSM8K, is competitive at moderate targets on MATH-500 and HumanEval, and largely transfers to Dream-7B-Instruct.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Research on sycophancy asks whether large language models (LLMs) bend towards what their users want to hear. We ask the reverse question: when an LLM is confident, consistent, and wrong, do users bend towards it? In a within-subjects experiment, 32 participants learned up to five science topics by chatting with five assistants built on the same model (GPT-5-mini). Unknown to participants, each assistant's briefing had 0, 25, 50, 75, or 100% of its facts replaced by plausible falsehoods, and every assistant spoke with full confidence. A surprise quiz after each topic offered the correct fact, the planted falsehood, and two distractors, with a confidence rating for every answer. Across 104 quizzes and 1,040 answers, scores fell from 7.4 to 2.9 out of 10, and the planted option was chosen on 60% of falsified items against 11% of correctly taught ones (odds ratio 12.5). Transcript coding showed that whenever the assistant stated a falsehood, it was adopted about 65% of the time at every falsification level. Absorbed falsehoods were held with high confidence (8.4/10), the modeled confidence advantage of right over wrong answers vanished, and the discrimination of confidence for correctness fell from an AUROC of 0.70 to chance, while post-task trust ratings barely moved. Participants tended to return to the chat more when the assistant was worse, but returning went with more absorption, not less, and 30% of all turns pasted quiz questions back to the source being checked. The assistant itself was almost never sycophantic and never conceded under pushback. We introduce a baseline-corrected Human Deference Index to quantify this human-side sycophancy and argue that trustworthiness is a property of the human-AI pair, that sycophancy evaluations should measure both directions, and that learning interfaces must make reliability legible claim by claim.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Control evaluations measure whether safeguards can prevent an AI model from causing undesired outcomes even when it is intentionally trying to do so. These evaluations typically use purpose-built control settings and extrapolate their results to a model's deployment. However, the effectiveness of safeguards in deployment can depend on features of environments and tasks that control settings do not represent. To broaden where control evaluations can be run, we introduce simulated control evaluations (SCEs), which use a language model to simulate tool responses in place of an environment. We evaluate SCEs on LinuxArena, where execution provides ground-truth outcomes. We compare SCE prediction errors with those from a separate control setting, BashArena, and with variation in control metrics within LinuxArena. For independently sampled evaluation and deployment task sets, SCEs predict safety with a root mean squared error of 3.6 percentage points across five monitors at a 5% audit budget, compared with 10.1 for BashArena. SCEs closely reproduce distributions of attack strategies, attacker reasoning, and monitor reasoning, with over tenfold lower KL divergence from execution than BashArena. SCEs explain 78-85% of the between-environment variance in attack success, monitor scores, and safety. This accuracy is sample efficient: SCEs predict safety with less error than BashArena using fewer than 5% as many task-pair draws as the deployment task set. Strategies discovered through simulation also improve monitor performance on held-out LinuxArena attacks, outperforming BashArena and approximately matching improvement from executed strategies. These results suggest that deployment-specific SCEs can be more predictive than control evaluations executed in separate control settings.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Tool-using language model agents rely on environmental facts to decide whether an action is permitted. When a tool response omits a safety-relevant fact, an agent may carry out a prohibited action even though the user request is benign and every returned statement is true. We introduce \bench{}, an executable benchmark for evaluating unsafe execution, safety boundaries, and evidence recovery in LLM agents under missing evidence. It contains 300 matched unsafe/safe task pairs across six domains. By systematically suppressing critical facts, the benchmark measures unsafe execution under critical evidence omission and determines the minimum observed failure cardinality, including failures with full evidence and cases with no observed failure after exhaustive testing. An independent audit tool tests whether agents seek missing facts and then withhold prohibited actions while completing permissible tasks. The benchmark reveals substantial variation across the ten models in omission robustness, evidence acquisition, and appropriate action. Resistance to missing evidence does not necessarily translate into useful recovery. Recovery access reduces unsafe execution for all ten models, but agents differ in whether they actively obtain the missing facts and use them to act appropriately in both worlds.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Test-time scaling has emerged as a key inference strategy for reasoning models, where a model generates a large number of candidate solutions in the hope of finding at least one correct solution. Running all candidate solutions to completion can be computationally demanding, so recent work prunes the candidate pool after generating only a short prefix, committing further computation only to the surviving candidates. Existing pruning strategies divide broadly into two categories: Quality-based methods assign a score to each partial solution and discard those predicted to ultimately lead to an incorrect final solution, while diversity-based methods select partial solutions that follow different reasoning trajectories and are therefore likely to cover different final solutions. The tension between these two objectives is an instance of exploration versus exploitation, yet little to no prior work on pruning exposes this trade-off directly, let alone optimizes it. In this paper, we introduce a pruning procedure that addresses this trade-off directly by optimizing pass@$k$ over the selected solution pool. In particular, it chooses the pool to maximize the probability that at least one of its partial solutions, when continued to completion, yields a correct answer. Because this objective simultaneously rewards high-quality partial solutions and penalizes redundancy among them, quality and diversity are naturally balanced by the criterion itself rather than through separate ad-hoc design choices. On eight open-weight reasoning models, our method outperforms existing quality-based and diversity-based pruning strategies. The gains are particularly pronounced when the pruning procedure keeps a moderate number of partial solutions for continued generation, highlighting the value of directly balancing quality and diversity during pruning.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The southeastern Tibetan Plateau (SETP), a critical component of the “Asian Water Tower”, hosts a large concentration of maritime temperate glaciers that are highly sensitive to climate change. However, persistent cloud cover, seasonal snow, and supraglacial debris complicate consistent long-term glacier mapping, while the use of static glacier boundaries may introduce temporal mismatches in altimetry-based glacier mass-change estimates. In this study, we developed a multi-source data-fusion framework on Google Earth Engine (GEE) to reconstruct glacier-area changes in the SETP from 2000 to 2025 at five-year intervals and 30 m spatial resolution. Random Forest classifiers were used to integrate Landsat and Sentinel-2 optical imagery, Sentinel-1 synthetic-aperture-radar data, and NASADEM-derived topographic information, supporting glacier delineation under complex surface and atmospheric conditions. The reconstructed glacier area decreased from 8,083 ± 664 km2 in 2000 to 6,228 ± 572 km2 in 2025, corresponding to a cumulative loss of 22.9 ± 3.6% and a mean loss rate of 80.51 ± 10.51 km2 yr−1. The mean area-loss rates were 51.31 ± 6.51 km2 yr−1 during 2000–2010 and 92.99 ± 15.55 km2 yr−1 during 2010–2025, indicating a higher average loss rate in the later period. By combining satellite-altimetry-derived elevation changes from ICESat, CryoSat-2, and ICESat-2 with the reconstructed time-varying glacier areas, we estimated a mean glacier mass-change rate of −6.51 ± 1.22 Gt yr−1 during 2003–2022. Compared with using a fixed 2003 glacier area, the time-varying-area calculation reduced the estimated mass-loss magnitude by 0.63 Gt yr−1, equivalent to 8.8% of the fixed-area estimate. These results indicate that incorporating time-varying glacier areas can reduce temporal mismatch in altimetry-based mass-change estimation and improve the temporal consistency of regional glacier mass-change assessments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spatial prediction is a fundamental task in geographic information science and environmental resource assessment. However, under complex terrain and heterogeneous covariates, models relying only on point-scale attributes or a single global relationship often have difficulty representing spatial context and regional heterogeneity. This study proposes a second-dimension-enhanced spatial context-aware modeling framework (SDA-SCA) that integrates first-dimension covariates, neighborhood-based second-dimension features, covariate screening, spatially constrained stratification, and local model training. Using two independently constructed annual spatial-prediction cases based on surface solar radiation observations from 670 stations in China in 2022 and 2023, five modeling schemes—Ordinary Kriging, Geographically Weighted Random Forest (GWRF), first-dimension attribute modeling (FDA), second-dimension augmented modeling (SDA), and SDA-SCA—were evaluated under spatial cross-validation. SDA-SCA showed a consistent predictive advantage across the two independent annual cases and the three evaluated model families. Relative to FDA, SDA-SCA increased R2 by 0.11–0.16 in 2022 and 0.17–0.21 in 2023, while reducing RMSE by 12.4%–17.7% and 24.3%–33.7%, and MAE by 22.8%–35.5% and 31.5%–50.4%, respectively. Stage-wise ablation showed that the FDA-to-SDA step produced consistent but moderate improvements, whereas the SDA-to-SDA-SCA step provided larger and more stable gains. These results indicate that second-dimension features improve the representation of neighborhood context, and their predictive value is further strengthened when coupled with spatial stratification. SHAP analysis further confirmed the substantial contribution of second-dimension features to the integrated model. The proposed framework provides an extensible pathway for spatial prediction tasks requiring the joint representation of local attributes, neighborhood context, and spatial heterogeneity.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mangroves are among the most important blue carbon ecosystems, contributing substantially to global carbon sequestration and biodiversity conservation. Canopy carbon (CCC), nitrogen (CNC), and potassium (CKC) contents provide critical indicators of nutrient cycling, ecosystem functioning, and ecological health. However, the structural complexity and strong spatial heterogeneity of mangroves present major obstacles to understanding the spectral response mechanisms and achieving reliable large-scale retrieval of these biochemical parameters. To address these limitations, we developed an interpretable retrieval framework that integrates causal analysis with physical constraints for reliable estimation of mangrove biochemical parameters. First, we proposed a Multi-Channel Chatterjee-Enhanced Correlation Spectrum Analysis (MC-ξ-CS) method that integrates causal discovery to systematically identify spectral bands causally associated with CCC, CNC, and CKC. For large-scale retrieval of mangrove canopy parameters, the identified causal relationships were transferred to satellite observations through a Global Angle-Divergence Spectral Transfer (GADT) framework, yielding robust estimation (R2 = 0.511–0.669). Subsequently, a Dual-Mechanism Guided Physics-Informed Neural Network (DM-PINN) was developed to synergistically integrate causal analysis and physical constraints, achieving robust retrieval performance (R2 = 0.604–0.83) for three representative mangrove species and enhancing the physical interpretability of the results. To the best of our knowledge, this study provides the first comprehensive large-scale retrieval of key mangrove biochemical parameters along the southeastern coast of China, revealing CCC values of 409.18–566.89 g/kg (C/N = 9.77–14.21), CNC values of 14.38–74.9 g/kg (N/K = 1.99–3.86), and CKC values of 4.78–19.67 g/kg (C/K = 28.22–37.67). Distinct inter-provincial spatial patterns were observed: CNC and CKC exhibited similar gradients, increasing from landward to seaward regions, whereas CCC displayed an opposite spatial distribution pattern with lower values in coastal regions. Moreover, CCC was strongly positively correlated with CNC, while CKC showed negative correlations with both parameters. Collectively, this study established a novel theoretical and methodological framework for elucidating the spectral response mechanisms of mangrove biochemical parameters, offering critical scientific support for large-scale mangrove degradation monitoring, ecological restoration assessment, and biodiversity conservation.

Abstract (英文): Long-term deformation of high earth-rockfill dams reflects post-construction consolidation, creep, reservoir loading, and cyclic operation. Separating these superimposed components is essential for gaining process-level insights into spatial patterns and differential responses. This study investigates the Pubugou Dam, a 186 m high earth-rockfill dam in Southwest China, using ascending and descending Sentinel-1 observations from 2017–2023. The two viewing geometries were jointly inverted to recover vertical and east-west displacements. A wavelet-guided decomposition, in which dominant periods identified by the continuous wavelet transform constrain the window length and component grouping in singular spectrum analysis, was proposed to separate the displacement records into long-term trend, annual cyclic, and residual components. Synthetic experiments yielded root mean square errors of 0.69–2.16 mm, indicating effective decomposition performance. Cross wavelet transform and wavelet transform coherence quantified localized coherence and phase lags between the cyclic components and reservoir water level. The central earth-rockfill body exhibits the largest magnitudes of vertical and east-west deformation rates, whereas the spillway and abutments remained comparatively stable. The trend components and settlement indices are consistent with relatively slow long-term post-construction settlement during the observation period. Annual cyclic deformation is more pronounced in the east-west direction and exhibits spatially variable lags relative to reservoir level fluctuations. Settlement inclination and apparent horizontal strain remain low under the adopted criteria, suggesting limited crest-cracking susceptibility during the observation period. This work demonstrates how wavelet-guided decomposition of two-dimensional InSAR time series provides spatially explicit insights into long-term settlement and cyclic deformation of high earth-rockfill dams.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A spectral proxy parameter linking remote sensing reflectance (Rrs) and inherent optical properties (IOPs) was evaluated for six selected areas of the Baltic Sea. Measured IOPs from the individual optical datasets were used to calculate Morel’s unscaled proxy reflectance, whereas area and wavelength specific median IOPs were used as representative inputs to Monte Carlo radiative transfer simulations. Comparison of the individual proxy estimates with the corresponding area-specific Monte Carlo reference reflectance enabled determination of the spectral proxy parameter k. The results demonstrate a clear wavelength dependence and substantial area to area variability of this parameter. The purpose of the study is not to introduce a new fundamental IOPs–Rrs relationship, but to quantify how the effective scaling coefficient of a widely used proxy relationship varies spectrally and regionally in optically complex Case-2 waters. For each investigated Baltic Sea area, the spectral variability of k was approximated using a three-coefficient second-order polynomial. Area-specific parameterizations provide compact descriptions of the regional median k spectra, while a generalized formulation describes only the pooled central tendency. A radiometric evaluation was performed using 24 in situ OCP-100 spectra from the Gulf of Gdańsk (N = 96 wavelength-level pairs). Predicted and measured Rrs yielded r = 0.680, R2 = 0.462, a regression slope of 0.258, a mean bias of −0.00475 sr−1, an RMSE of 0.00590 sr−1, and a MAPE of 62.1%. Within-wavelength correlations were weak at 555 and 620 nm (r = 0.140 and 0.067, respectively). Thus, the regional parameterization preserves broad spectral ordering but underestimates absolute Rrs and does not establish strong station-level predictive skill. The results should be interpreted as a regional, configuration-dependent spectral scaling framework.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Object detection in high-resolution remote sensing imagery faces a resolution–context trade-off: resizing weakens small objects, whereas cropping can truncate targets and remove context. We present QASHI, a detector-retraining-free hybrid inference framework combining a full-image view, four overlapping tiles, and at most three additional regions of interest (ROIs). Quality-Aware Density (QAD) prioritizes local re-inference by combining small-candidate density with weak responses, candidate competition, and full/tile prediction inconsistency. Graph-based ranking correction, bounded BoxVote refinement, and scale-protected routing integrate local predictions with full-image context. Experiments cover VisDrone2019-DET, DIOR, SeaDronesSee v2, and SRSDD-v1.0 with two RT-DETR detectors. On VisDrone-val with RT-DETRv4-S, QASHI improves AP by 2.84 points over the SAHI baseline while reducing mean latency by 14.40%. Relative to Full, it gains 5.74 AP points at 4.52 times the latency. Selector ablations and local parameter analyses characterize how region priority and fusion settings affect detection accuracy and computational cost. On SRSDD, display-domain image-quality sensitivity tests with RT-DETR-R50 yield higher mean perturbed AP (32.52 versus 31.41), with relative AP retention of 93.44% versus 93.98%. QASHI combines prediction-guided local processing and source-aware fusion to improve remote sensing detection through inference-time use of a fixed detector.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Soil moisture is a critical regulator in Earth’s energy cycle, exerting substantial influence on hydrological, ecological, and thermal processes. However, the spatio-temporal heterogeneity of soil moisture poses significant challenges for remote-sensing inversion, and conventional methods are constrained under complex surface conditions. In this study, Sentinel-1 and Sentinel-2 imagery were integrated with terrain data, and machine learning algorithms were employed to develop a soil moisture estimation model for the agro-pastoral transition zone. The primary objectives were to evaluate the accuracy of machine learning approaches for remote-sensing estimation of soil moisture under complex surface conditions, and to assess the suitability of different machine learning algorithms for this application. The results showed that ensemble methods, specifically boosting approaches, delivered low inversion errors and high R2 values, whereas linear models showed limited accuracy. The vertically polarized microwave backscattering coefficients emerged as the parameters most sensitive to soil moisture variations, with optical bands and topographic factors also making substantial contributions. The overall inversion yielded a root mean square error (RMSE) of 0.066 cm3.cm−3. This study provides an effective approach for soil moisture inversion on complex surfaces, simplifies the modeling process, and offers a tool for soil moisture monitoring across large spatial scales.

</details>

Abstract (英文): One-dimensional canopy radiative transfer models are widely used for leaf area index (LAI) retrieval, but their horizontally homogeneous representation can inadequately describe crown clumping, canopy gaps, woody elements, and shadowing in structurally complex forests. Explicit three-dimensional (3D) models offer greater structural realism, but their computational cost limits direct application at regional-to-continental scales. This study evaluates whether a residual correction distilled from the LargE-Scale remote sensing data and image simulation framework (LESS) 3.x into a PROSPECT + SAIL (PROSAIL) emulator improves forest overstory LAI identifiability when the inversion already has Global Ecosystem Dynamics Investigation (GEDI) relative-height metrics, canopy cover, and vertical-shape descriptors. The high-fidelity simulation library contains 3980 LESS scenes with explicit crowns and trunks (excluding understory and branches). Recomputing PROSAIL at the same Sentinel-2 band-center wavelengths yields a near-infrared 1D–3D reflectance residual of about −0.17, confirming a substantial structural discrepancy not attributable to spectral-response-function mismatch. On a structure-blocked split (2699 training and 1281 held-out validation scenes), with GEDI plant area index (PAI) excluded, the 1D inversion (E2) and multi-fidelity inversion (E5) use identical three-member ensembles and achieve LAI root-mean-square errors (RMSEs) of 1.564 and 1.563, respectively (R2 = 0.52). Block-bootstrap equivalence analysis yields a median ΔRMSE of −0.0009 with a 95% confidence interval of [−0.0025, −0.0001], entirely within the pre-specified ±0.05 LAI equivalence margin. The 3D residual reduces inverse spectral reconstruction RMSE from 0.038 to 0.034, but does not produce a practically meaningful LAI gain. An auxiliary joint LAI–clumping experiment is not identifiable (clumping R2 = −2.08), supporting treatment of clumping as a diagnostic rather than a retrieved product. Independent validation against Ground-Based Observations for Validation (GBOV) RM-7 Warren overstory LAI (187 observations, 16 sites) gives R2 = 0.30, r = 0.63, RMSE = 1.36, and bias = +0.16. These results show that improved 3D forward-model fidelity does not necessarily improve parameter identifiability when independent structural constraints are already present.

</details>
