Abstract (英文): The collaborative classification of hyperspectral images (HSI) with a complementary modality such as light detection and ranging (LiDAR) or synthetic aperture radar (SAR), which is known as multi-source remote sensing image classification (MRSIC), can combine rich spectral information with structural cues and is helpful for the recognition of complex ground objects. However, effectively incorporating these structural cues into multi-level spectral-spatial features and modeling their spatial relationships remain challenging under limited training samples. To address these problems, a multi-level convolutional collaborative classification network based on the DefMamba fusion mechanism (DMFNet) is proposed in this paper. First, monogenic phase congruency (MPC) responses are added to the complementary modality to enhance its structural information. Then, a dual-branch feature extraction module is constructed to derive multi-level features from HSI and the enhanced complementary modality and aggregate them into a joint representation. Finally, a DefMamba fusion module applies deformable selective scanning to the aggregated features, so as to adaptively model spatial dependencies according to the fused content. Experiments on three public multimodal remote sensing datasets, covering both HSI–LiDAR and HSI–SAR settings, demonstrate that DMFNet achieves the highest mean overall accuracy among the compared methods with 10 labeled training samples per class, with the largest improvement on the HSI-SAR scene. Additional experiments on Houston 2018 further evaluate its classification performance on a large-scale scene.

</details>

Abstract (英文): As a fundamental component of the Earth’s climate system, snow cover critically mediates surface radiative forcing, terrestrial water storage, and large-scale atmospheric momentum exchange. Its long-term trends are a direct fingerprint of global warming, yet the scientific literature on snow cover dynamics has grown exponentially, posing substantial challenges for systematic knowledge synthesis. To map the intellectual landscape of Northern Hemisphere (NH) snow cover dynamics, we conducted a comprehensive bibliometric review of this corpus based on 10,350 peer-reviewed articles retrieved from the Web of Science. The bibliometric analysis reveals a sustained upward trajectory in publication output, with core journals concentrated in Remote Sensing, Journal of Geophysical Research: Atmospheres, and The Cryosphere, and leading research institutions represented by the Chinese Academy of Sciences, the French National Centre for Scientific Research, and the University of Colorado Boulder. Methodologically, this field is shifting toward the integration of high-resolution remote sensing with intelligent algorithms. Geographically, research hotspots are predominantly located in high-mountain areas, with additional focus on typical snow zones across North America, Europe, Russia, Central Asia, and the Tibetan Plateau. Despite ongoing advances in remote sensing and algorithmic techniques, the synergistic integration of multi-source snow data and the operational production of long-term, high-resolution hemispheric snow cover products remain critical bottlenecks that urgently need to be addressed. These findings provide valuable references for optimizing future snow monitoring systems and advancing model development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Tibetan Plateau (TP) hosts the world’s largest and highest-altitude plateau lake system, whose ecosystems are highly sensitive to climate change. Chlorophyll-a (Chl-a), as a key indicator of phytoplankton biomass and lake trophic status, exhibits pronounced changes in response to long-term climatic and environmental changes. However, knowledge of its seasonal variations in TP lakes remains limited due to a shortage of high-time-resolution analyses based on in situ observations and remote sensing inversion. In this study, we compiled in situ Chl-a data from 95 lakes and combined them with Moderate Resolution Imaging Spectroradiometer (MODIS) ocean-color bands (B8–B16) and the Climate Change Initiative (CCI) Chl-a product to develop a back-propagation neural network model specifically for oligotrophic plateau lakes. The model demonstrated excellent performance across training, testing, and independent validation datasets (R2 = 0.82–0.84; RMSE = 0.44–0.49 μg L−1), confirming its suitability for regional-scale retrievals. Independent external evaluation using 24 observations from 8 TP lakes excluded from model development yielded R2 = 0.85 and RMSE = 0.24 μg L−1. Using this model, we reconstructed Chl-a concentrations during the ice-free period (26 May to 9 December) from 2000 to 2022 for 163 lakes larger than 50 km2 and systematically analyzed their seasonal dynamics within the ice-free period and controlling factors. The mean Chl-a concentration across all study lakes was 1.43 μg L−1, with concentrations significantly higher in autumn (September–November) than in summer (June–August). Fuzzy c-means clustering classified the seasonal dynamics into two groups: Class I lakes (94 lakes, 60%), peaking in August and mainly located in the westerlies-dominated region (88%), were primarily influenced by air temperature, solar radiation, and water temperature; whereas Class II lakes (69 lakes, 40%), peaking in November and predominantly distributed in the monsoon-dominated region (72%), were mainly driven by wind speed and mixed-layer depth. From 2000 to 2022, the timing of Chl-a peaks exhibited contrasting trends, advancing in Class I lakes and delaying in Class II lakes. These results showed the ability of remote sensing to monitor seasonal Chl-a dynamics and regional differentiation in the TP lakes, and their underlying climatic and limnological drivers, as well as high-altitude lake responses to global climate change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This study investigates the potential of integrated multispectral and thermal UAV remote sensing for detecting and mapping hidden archaeological structures of Proto-Colchian and Colchian settlement systems of the 3rd–1st millennium BCE, within the Rioni River basin in western Georgia. Characteristic settlement mounds (Dikhagudzuba), moats/ditches, and possible paleochannel networks represent a unique example of large-scale adaptation to waterlogged environments, and limited archaeological excavations have significantly hindered the identification and interpretation of these buried landscape features. Pilot UAV surveys were conducted at four archaeological sites differing in location, geomorphological setting, land use, and degree of preservation using DJI Mavic 3M multispectral and DJI Matrice 4T thermal UAV platforms. Multispectral imagery enabled the detection of vegetation and soil-moisture anomalies associated with buried ditches, embankments, canals, and settlement structures, while thermal data revealed differences in thermal inertia related to subsurface archaeological remains and palaeohydrological features. The study covered approximately 15.4 ha and produced 5836 RGB, 2219 multispectral, and 3617 thermal images with spatial resolutions ranging from 1.87 to 4.61 cm. The integration of high-resolution elevation models, RGB imagery, vegetation indices (ExG and normalized EVI2), and thermal data enabled the identification and characterization of microtopographic, vegetation, moisture, and thermal anomalies associated with archaeological and palaeohydrological features. The investigated mounds cover 2754–4685 m2, reach approximately 9–10 m in height, and are surrounded by moats/ditches covering 4265–6520 m2 and ramparts reaching approximately 2–3.5 m in height. The results demonstrate that combined UAV-based multispectral and thermal remote sensing provides a highly effective non-invasive tool for archaeological prospection in dynamic wetland landscapes. The proposed approach significantly improves the detection of buried archaeological features and paleochannels, while also supporting landscape-scale analyses, monitoring of archaeological heritage, and the development of predictive archaeological models for western Georgia.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In resource-based cities of arid regions, ecosystem carbon sequestration capacity is shaped by the interactive effects of climate change, resource exploitation, and ecological restoration. Quantitatively evaluating the dynamics of carbon sink capacity and its driving mechanisms is essential for unraveling carbon cycle processes in coupled human–environment systems, enhancing carbon sequestration efficiency, and advancing regional carbon neutrality and peaking targets. Taking Ordos City, a representative resource-based city in an arid region, as a case study, this study integrates an improved CASA model, a soil respiration model, trend analyses, stability analyses, partial correlation analysis, and the Optimal Parameters-based Geographical Detector (OPGD) to investigate the spatiotemporal dynamics, future trajectories, and influencing factors of net ecosystem productivity (NEP) in resource-based cities located in arid areas. The main findings are as follows: (1) From 2001 to 2023, NEP in Ordos City showed a fluctuating upward trend, with a spatial pattern characterized by “higher values in the east and lower values in the west”. Notably, some areas have successfully transitioned from carbon sources to carbon sinks. (2) Over the past 23 years, NEP has demonstrated a pronounced increasing trend with strong spatial heterogeneity and is projected to maintain continuous growth in the future. Areas characterized by sustained growth account for as much as 63.22% of the total region, indicating that the regional carbon sink capacity is strengthening and will continue to rise. (3) The explanatory power of factors influencing the spatial heterogeneity of NEP varies significantly, and the interactive effects among multiple factors have been greatly enhanced. Population density (q = 0.72) and evapotranspiration (q = 0.63) exhibit the strongest explanatory power for the spatial differentiation of NEP, whereas the explanatory power of the distance to mining areas has been continuously increasing. (4) Future research should prioritize the interactive coupling effects between natural factors and human activities, establish a compensation mechanism for carbon sink losses induced by mining development, strengthen the synergistic mechanisms linking ecological restoration and carbon sink enhancement, and focus on preventing and mitigating the risk of ecological carbon sink degradation triggered by unregulated urban spatial expansion.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing combined with machine learning (ML) and deep learning (DL) is widely used to estimate tropical forest aboveground biomass (AGB), yet it is unclear how fully published studies document the evidence that Measurement, Reporting and Verification (MRV) of forest carbon would require. We assessed this reporting evidence using a five-dimension framework (accuracy reporting, uncertainty quantification, spatial validation, temporal consistency and transparency) applied to a systematically identified set of empirical studies. From 1706 records retrieved from Scopus and Lens.org on 4 March 2026, 1146 unique works were screened against a locked eligibility codebook; 153 reports were assessed in full text where accessible, and 59 empirical ML/DL remote-sensing AGB studies in tropical or subtropical forests formed the core review set. For each dimension we coded evidence as confirmed, reported as absent, insufficient or inaccessible, and report the share of studies between the confirmed count and the confirmed-plus-undetermined count. Accuracy metrics were reported in 66–88% of studies, but spatially independent validation was confirmed in only 32–42%, spatially explicit or area-level uncertainty in 44–54%, multi-date analysis with an explicit consistency method in 14–22%, public code in 14–29% and public reference data in 15–29%. Only 12 studies confirmed both independent validation and propagated uncertainty. The results describe the evidence reported in research papers and do not assess compliance of any product with a carbon standard. Screening and evidence extraction were AI-assisted against a fixed codebook with a verbatim evidence phrase for every decision, and a stratified sample of 60 screening decisions was independently verified by two co-authors (agreement 88% and 78%). We recommend that biomass studies intended to inform MRV report spatially blocked or independent-site validation, propagate uncertainty to area totals, and release code and reference data.

</details>

During the 1999–2001 drawdown, open-water area fell from a pre-event mean of 1061 ha to 64.9 ha on 3 July 2000, a loss of 93.9%. Comparison with in situ stage across 56 paired observations yielded an empirical stage–area relationship (R2 = 0.67; RMSE = 136 ha). Coverage-threshold sensitivity and precipitation-lag screening informed the subsequent evaluation design. That design specifies four lake-surface classes, objective event extraction, statistical and machine-learning baselines, spatially structured challengers, leave-one-drawdown-event-out validation, and a prespecified model-promotion rule. Abstract (英文): Recurrent Ulva prolifera (U. prolifera) green tides in the Yellow Sea have become a persistent coastal ecological problem, yet their long-term phenological changes and responses to environmental variability remain insufficiently understood. Here, we reconstructed the spatiotemporal dynamics of U. prolifera from 2008 to 2025 using multi-source satellite observations. Floating algae were detected using the Floating Algae Index (FAI) or Alternative Floating Algae Index (AFAI), and subpixel coverage was estimated using linear spectral unmixing. The results showed that MODIS-derived green tide extent expanded markedly, reaching 886.13 km2 in 2021. June exhibited the strongest long-term increasing trend. VIIRS observations showed broadly similar spatial and interannual bloom patterns over 2016–2025, while hourly GOCI-II observations revealed pronounced intraday variability which coverage generally increasing from morning to noon or afternoon. Environmental analyses demonstrated that bloom variability was jointly regulated by multiple environmental factors, including nonlinear relationships with sea surface temperature, pH, and dissolved oxygen, as well as linear effects of salinity and silicate. The multivariable model explained 70.7% of the observed variation in bloom area, highlighting the importance of coupled physical and biogeochemical controls. These findings indicate that Yellow Sea green tides are becoming increasingly persistent under changing environmental conditions. Multi-source satellite monitoring can inform adaptive management that combines nature-based solutions (NbS), including source-region nutrient reduction, optimized aquaculture structures, ecological polyculture with competitive macroalgae, hydrodynamic-based interception, and sustainable biomass utilization. Such approaches may reduce bloom risks while enhancing coastal ecosystem resilience and transforming U. prolifera management from passive removal toward proactive ecological regulation.

</details>

One potential factor is the expansion of coniferous forests, which intercept snow that sublimates to the atmosphere rather than reaching streams. We evaluated the increase in coniferous cover in the lake tributary basins and quantified the potential sublimation water loss. In Google Earth Engine we built annual summer–winter Landsat composites (14 features: six reflectance bands and NDVI for each season, 1986–2025) and classified coniferous forest using a 100-member Random Forest ensemble, giving an annual uncertainty envelope with a median run-to-run spread of 4.3%. We assessed trends with the Mann–Kendall test and Sen’s slope and estimated canopy sublimation as a fraction of snowfall. Coniferous cover in the Bear, Weber, and Jordan River tributary basins increased significantly over 1986–2025 (Sen’s slope +27 km2 yr−1, p = 0.00064; trend-based increase 1059 km2, about 35% of the 1986 extent). Applying Utah canopy-sublimation fractions (5–11% of snowfall) and a mean snowfall water equivalent of 0.71 m, the additional forest sublimates an estimated 30,000–67,000 acre-feet per year. While this is roughly 1.4–3.2% of mean annual river inflow, only a portion of the snowfall contributes to river flow, so the actual impact is lower, although loss is linear with snowfall.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Object detection in remote sensing imagery is a fundamental and crucial task within the remote sensing domain. However, small object detection in remote sensing images remains a challenge for SOTA methods. This is because small objects often have large aspect ratios, exhibit characteristic variations across different orientations, and are distributed within complex backgrounds. Current methods, which employ square convolutions, often retain excessive background information during feature extraction, leading to suboptimal detection performance. In this paper, an orthogonal-nesting orientation-aware small object detection network based on YOLOv10 is proposed to achieve small object detection in complex backgrounds. ONOA-YOLO employs an orthogonal-nesting module to enhance the features of small objects from multiple orientations, improving the sensitivity of the network to their intrinsic features. Additionally, it incorporates a background-suppression module based on large convolutional kernels. This module integrates the contextual information of small objects as guiding cues with other features, enabling the suppression of background interference while preserving the extracted small object features.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In this work, we compare deep convolutional and generative models to improve the reconstruction of sea surface temperature (SST) gradients and effective resolution from cloud-contaminated satellite infrared data. Standard gap-filling techniques produce spatially complete datasets, but they inherently smooth out fine-scale features and gradients that may be critical for a better understanding of the ocean dynamics. Here, we investigate how the use of deep learning models as Autoencoders (AEs) and generative models as Conditional Generative Adversarial Networks (C-GANs) differently contribute to recovering the small-scale structures lost during interpolation. Our supervised, observation-based training relies on satellite-derived SST data from the Mediterranean Sea, with a focus on learning the statistical relationship between high-resolution fields and their low-resolution counterparts. We apply a tiling and merging strategy to deal with limited observational coverage and to ensure spatial continuity. Quantitative validation based on mean squared error metrics, spectral analysis, and gradient statistics shows that the AE reduces the reconstruction error, but it fails to recover high-frequency variability. In contrast, the C-GAN effectively restores the statistical properties of the true SST field at the cost of increasing the pointwise discrepancy with the ground truth observations. Our results highlight the potential of deep models to enhance the physical and statistical realism of gap-filled satellite data in oceanographic applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To extend the scale limits within which a template watermark remains detectable, this paper proposes a template watermarking algorithm for remote sensing images based on a radial spectral barcode (RSB). The RSB is a new geometry of template watermark whose inner and outer boundaries are separated along the radial direction of the discrete Fourier transform magnitude spectrum. In the embedding stage, the spectrum is divided into angular slots, a synchronization sequence generated from the secret key and the watermark are assigned to alternate slots, and the magnitude within the radial interval of each slot is modulated according to its sign. In the detection stage, the angular profile of the received spectrum is computed under a set of geometric hypotheses, the synchronization sequence is located by circular correlation, and the watermark is read from the synchronized profile without correcting the image. The experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method extends the detectable scale interval well beyond that of the ring-template methods while retaining robustness against rotation, translation, cropping, affine transformation, map reprojection, and signal-processing attacks. It outperforms the comparative algorithms in robustness at an equal or higher PSNR, with a low false detection rate.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Floating debris threatens aquatic ecosystems, yet detecting it in close-range aquatic remote sensing imagery remains difficult because small, irregular, or partially submerged objects resemble specular highlights and ripples. This study proposes FLNet, a YOLO11-L-based detector for water-surface monitoring from unmanned surface vehicles (USVs). FLNet redesigns the backbone, neck, and head with three components: a Dynamic Polarity-Aware Robustness Block that calibrates deep activations and models similar and opposing feature relations; an Adaptive Multi-Receptive Pyramid that repeatedly fuses three adjacent scales through multi-branch depthwise convolutions; and a Detail-Preserving Shared Head that applies one boundary-sensitive prediction trunk to all scales. On a custom five-category dataset of 12,237 images, FLNet achieved 85.93% macro-Precision, 85.08% macro-F1, and 88.77% mean average Precision at an intersection-over-union threshold of 0.5 (mAP@0.5), the highest single-run values among the evaluated detectors, with 24M parameters and 83 GFLOPs. Across three random seeds, its macro-Precision and macro-F1 advantages over the strongest competitor, Hyper-YOLO, exceeded seed-to-seed variability, whereas its mAP@0.5 was statistically comparable to that of Hyper-YOLO and Hyper-YOLO retained a higher mAP@0.5:0.95. In separately trained benchmarks, FLNet achieved the highest F1-score on FloW-Img (80.26%) and the highest macro-F1 on MarineDebris-yolo (79.82%). With TensorRT half-precision (FP16) acceleration, detector-side inference reached 29.8, 111.2, and 56.4 frames per second on Jetson Xavier NX, AGX Orin, and Orin Nano Super, respectively. FLNet therefore offers a favorable accuracy–efficiency balance for close-range floating-debris detection.

Abstract (英文): The transfer of precise reference brightness temperature (BT) from the microwave calibration target (MCT) to the receiver antenna is a significant and fundamental issue in achieving accurate microwave radiometer calibration. In addition to the issues of receiver nonlinearity, gain stability et al., this review focuses on the electromagnetic field propagation process. Addressing the precise brightness temperature transfer from the MCT to the receiving antenna in the near-field region, this review systematically summarizes recent research progress by domestic and international scholars on the electromagnetic and thermal emission characteristics of MCT, as well as brightness temperature transfer modeling methodologies and their intrinsic properties. Or in other words, from the perspectives of (1) the accurate BT emission from the MCT, and (2) the accurate transferring in the calibration link, as they are the physical foundation of the radiometer calibration process and corresponding problems took decades to be tackled. This review aims to provide a comprehensive reference encompassing theories, methodologies, and fundamental mechanisms for the progressive establishment of microwave brightness temperature standards and transfer standards by discussing specific issues regarding how MCTs can be accurately characterized and how the BT can be precisely transferred.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Efficient planning of forest roads is essential to sustaining uninterrupted forestry activities and supporting sustainable forest management. Roads must be designed in concert with existing infrastructure while conforming to operational requirements for timber extraction, accessibility, and environmental compliance. Advances in high-resolution remote sensing and computational geomatics enable acquisition of high-precision, three-dimensional descriptions of forest structure integrable into geographic information systems (GISs) for route optimization and terrain modeling. Despite this progress, a formal procedure for designing forest roads from point clouds derived by combining aerial and terrestrial laser scanning is not yet established. This study addresses that gap by presenting a complete, reproducible workflow for forest road design using fused airborne and near-ground laser scanning point clouds. The workflow encompasses three stages: LiDAR acquisition and derivation of topographic and vegetation attributes, optimal route identification, and estimation of standing timber volume within the proposed corridor. The procedure is demonstrated on a 200 m test section within the McDonald-Dunn Research Forest of Oregon State University, where 133 trees were inventoried and cut and fill volumes quantified. The results confirm that LiDAR-based planning achieves sub-0.12 m accuracy while enabling precise quantification of standing stock to be removed, reducing field time, improving cost efficiency, and supporting data-driven environmental decision-making.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery plays an important role in disaster monitoring, environmental monitoring, and related applications. However, the acquisition of measured SAR data is limited by high costs and the difficulty of covering diverse environmental conditions, and the volume of SAR imagery is far smaller than that of optical remote sensing imagery, which restricts learning-based SAR applications. This paper proposes a multi-view SAR image simulation method based on OptiX and residual learning. The proposed method uses the NVIDIA OptiX ray-tracing API to simulate electromagnetic wave propagation, uses Monte Carlo sampling to model material interactions and coherent summation to form SAR images, and generates multi-view SAR image sequences of buildings. The simulator uses a scalar multiple-bounce approximation, and simulation, refinement, and evaluation use only the HH channel. The OptiX-based simulator is coupled with a residual U-Net that predicts the residual between simulated and measured images and compensates for missing fine-scale details. The method supports efficient multi-view generation. Quantitative validation on the SARMV3D dataset shows that the refined simulated images achieve low pixel-level error and high structural and perceptual similarity, yielding average values of 23.88 dB for the peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR), 0.83 for the structural similarity index measure (SSIM), and 0.20 for learned perceptual image patch similarity (LPIPS). The method provides an effective means for generating multi-view SAR images of buildings, has potential for use in downstream tasks such as target recognition, is physically interpretable, and shows promising generalization capability across the evaluated building scenes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate estimation of stratified forest canopy height is crucial for understanding forest dynamics and estimating carbon stocks. While remote sensing is widely used for regional canopy mapping, its accuracy is often limited in mountainous and dense forests, particularly for understory canopy layers, because of signal saturation and restricted sensor penetration through the overstory canopy. To address these issues, we design a novel deep learning model, which combines the self-attention mechanism and the Vision Transformer encoder–decoder architecture (ViT-ed) with conditional modeling to achieve precise prediction of stratified canopy height using multi-temporal Sentinel-1/2 and Global Ecosystem Dynamics Investigation (GEDI) data. This model structure is tailored to the ecological correlations between vertical forest layers and optimized with a hybrid loss function and achieves significant improvements over the encoder-only convolutional neural network baseline (CNN-e). Validated against field inventory data, our model achieves a correlation coefficient of 0.843 (root mean square error (RMSE) = 1.532 m) for overstory canopy height and 0.825 (RMSE = 0.377 m) for understory canopy height, markedly outperforming the CNN-e benchmarks (RMSE = 1.846 m and 0.451 m, respectively). These results demonstrate improved understory canopy height estimation relative to CNN-e in the study area. Based on freely available data, this method generates continuous canopy height maps for the study area and has potential to enhance carbon stock estimation across broader regions.

Abstract (英文): Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) products from the European Ground Motion Service (EGMS) are released through successive, partially overlapping temporal windows, offering the potential to construct ultra-long-term (ULT) deformation records, i.e., continuous records spanning multiple releases and exceeding the 5–6-year extent of any individual release. However, direct combination of independently processed releases may introduce artificial offsets and trend changes. This study presents an enhanced Temporal Alignment via Statistical Testing (TAST) procedure to harmonize four EGMS Ortho releases (R1–R4; January 2016–December 2024) over the Lecco–Como area of northern Italy in both Up–Down and East–West components. The method applies translation and linear velocity correction optimized through a joint Cramér–von Mises/RMSE (Root Mean Square Error) objective while preserving within-release deformation evolution. The resulting ULT records were segmented at three regional groundwater-level (GWL) turning points (TPs) and analyzed using the introduced Hydro-Deformation Regime Concordance (HDRC) metric. The results show that inter-release velocity discrepancies reached 7.12 mm/y in U-D and 11.21 mm/y in E-W, demonstrating their potential to substantially alter apparent long-term deformation regimes. Vertical deformation was predominantly anti-concordant with GWL turning behavior, with 59.7–69.9% negative HDRC, whereas E-W responses evolved from strong positive HDRC dominance to near-balanced conditions. HDRC exhibited significant spatial organization (Moran’s I up to 0.51) and geological association (Cramér’s up to 0.31), while geological-group response rankings remained persistent across successive hydro-regime transitions (Kendall’s W up to 0.88). A screening of potential alternative regional drivers identified no clear evidence that seismicity, capable faults, or isolated extreme precipitation events accounted for the observed multi-TP patterns. These results demonstrate that systematic multi-release EGMS harmonization enables temporally consistent long-term monitoring and reveals non-stationary, spatially structured, and geologically conditioned deformation responses associated with groundwater dynamics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing systems, and X-band marine radar (MR) specifically, enable the acquisition of directional wave spectra (DWS) over a wide spatial coverage by processing backscatter images. It has been shown that the MR look azimuth has a significant impact on the accuracy of the resulting DWS. While the effect of azimuthal dependency is observed in both deep and shallow water, it becomes critical for measurements in coastal environments, where spectral properties change frequently due to wave refraction, reflection, and dissipation. In this study, shore-based MR data are used to process DWS and integrated wave parameters across different wave regimes. The dependence of the DWS uncertainties on the look azimuth is evaluated via comparison with concurrent Acoustic Doppler Current Profiler (ADCP) measurements. We propose a retrieval strategy that composites multiple sub-images sampled at different look azimuths along a common isobath, so that every window captures the same stage of wave shoaling, for a more accurate evaluation of DWS and integrated wave parameters. The approach assumes an alongshore-uniform bathymetry, a condition met at the present site and common to gently sloping sandy coasts, and its applicability to irregular shorelines is considered in the discussion. The developed composite approach enhances the ability of the MR to accurately resolve sea-state directionality even in the case of multiple interacting wave systems. Cross-validation against the ADCP shows that the retrieved mean wave direction can differ by up to 30 between extreme look azimuths, and that the composite scheme recovers directionality with the best-balanced error metrics across the tested methods. These results argue against reliance on any single MR window for coastal directional monitoring. This advancement is essential for coastal engineering applications, as even directional misinterpretations of a few degrees can significantly alter the estimates of longshore sediment transport.

</details>

This study proposes a novel spatiotemporal zoning strategy to reconstruct GOME-2 SIF. The Spatiotemporal Ensemble (STE) model integrates multi-source geospatial data and diverse ML models, demonstrating superior SIF estimation performance. The STE model achieved an R2 of 0.925 with 5-fold cross-validation and an RMSE of 0.058 mW m−2 sr−1 nm−1, outperforming six classical ML models by 14%. Temporally, the seasonal variability in model performance was reduced, with the RMSE gap between months with peak and trough errors decreasing to merely 0.04 mW m−2 sr−1 nm−1. This study investigates the Gimigliano landslide (Calabria, southern Italy), a large, long-lived and urbanized slope instability, by integrating multi-temporal Sentinel-1 SBAS-InSAR observations, data-driven kinematic segmentation, geomorphological analysis and rainfall–deformation relationships. InSAR time series were clustered to identify spatially coherent kinematic domains, which were subsequently compared with independently mapped geomorphological sectors. Continuous and event-based analyses were then used to investigate the temporal relationship between rainfall and landslide deformation. Four kinematic domains were identified, with the highest domain-averaged deformation rates reaching approximately −12.8 mm/yr in the vertical component and −4.8 mm/yr in the east–west component. The identified domains show a clear spatial correspondence with the geomorphological elements of the landslide. Rainfall–deformation relationships showed the highest-magnitude correlations for 60–120 days rainfall accumulation windows, with maximum correlations occurring at lags of approximately 30–35 days for the vertical component and 60–75 days for the east–west component. Event-based analysis further highlighted variable response magnitudes among domains and delayed responses within a few weeks. The results indicate that deformation is associated with antecedent hydrological conditions while retaining substantial spatial heterogeneity, highlighting the value of integrating InSAR, geomorphological and rainfall information for the interpretation of slow-moving landslide dynamics.

The framework provides a practical approach for generating high-spatial-resolution precipitation estimates in the Yellow River Basin.

Abstract (英文): Kelp forests are vital marine habitats with significant ecological, cultural, and economic importance. These ecosystems, found along coastlines, are susceptible to regional and global stressors including coastal development and climate change. This paper presents Satellite-based Kelp Mapping (SKeMa), a novel framework for automatically mapping canopy-forming kelp forests along the coast of British Columbia using Sentinel-2 satellite scenes. SKeMa employs a deep learning semantic segmentation model, offering an efficient alternative to traditional, labor-intensive, and time-consuming kelp-mapping methods. Here, we initially evaluated SKeMa against two conventional, non-deep-learning baselines, NDVI thresholding and Random Forest, and several alternative deep learning architectures. For the best-performing model, an ablation study on input channels identifies a suitable input configuration, and a cross-validation study with independent test sets yields a mean Intersection over Union (IoU) of 0.6024 and F1-score of 0.7491. Average precision and recall are 0.7261 and 0.7951, respectively. Overall, the highest validation metrics were for images with medium to large kelp beds and the lowest for images with small kelp beds, likely because Sentinel-2’s spatial resolution limits mapping of small kelp beds. These results are achieved under a highly imbalanced class distribution, with labeled kelp pixels constituting, on average, less than 0.1% of all pixels in a Sentinel-2 scene. The results demonstrate the model’s ability to detect kelp canopies across diverse coastal regions, particularly for medium to large kelp beds.

Abstract (英文): Remote sensing captions typically interleave domain-specific terminology, object attributes, spatial relations, and scene context within a single sentence, while the corresponding imagery contains targets spanning markedly different spatial extents—from individual vehicles to entire airport layouts. A single global text vector and fixed-scale visual pooling therefore tend to discard the local and hierarchical evidence needed for reliable image–text alignment. Moreover, standard contrastive objectives treat all unmatched pairs as equally unrelated, ignoring the fact that semantically adjacent scenes (e.g., airports and industrial zones) share substantial visual content. To address these issues, we develop a CLIP-style dual encoder with three targeted additions: a text encoder that preserves word-level, phrase-level, and sentence-level representations before fusing them through cross-granularity attention; a visual encoder that associates four hierarchical feature levels with scale-aware receptive-field configurations and incorporates SAM-guided local views during training; and a semantic membership-aware contrastive loss that applies detached, bounded discount weights to semantically close negatives without inverting their penalty sign. On the cleaned RSICD benchmark, the model achieves 87.5% mAP for in-domain prompt-based scene recognition and 34.37% mean recall for scene-constrained retrieval, outperforming the quoted RemoteCLIP and fully fine-tuned CLIP results in the scene-constrained retrieval comparison. Ablation confirms that each of the three components contributes meaningfully to the final performance. The deployable dual encoder contains approximately 59.7 million trainable parameters, and SAM is not required at inference.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Lakes are crucial markers of climate fluctuations, yet identifying water bodies in mountainous regions remains a persistent challenge for satellite remote sensing due to terrain-induced obstacles and dynamic surface conditions of ice and snow. We present a novel approach based solely on Sentinel-1 Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) data that allows for the detection of lakes as well as the tracking of their surface area and conditions in mountainous terrain. Our methodology is built around a new index called OASIS, which uses pairs of SAR images to detect and monitor lake surface conditions. We show that this index provides critical insights into lake surface conditions, including the onset and duration of ice cover and the observation of frozen lakes concealed beneath snow. We evaluate this capacity using in situ lake temperature measurements at Arpont Lake. At this lake, the estimated open-water onset typically occurs in mid- to late July, with an ice-free season lasting roughly three to three and a half months. The years 2017 and especially 2022 stand out, showing an earlier break-up (2 July and 24 June, respectively) and longer open-water periods (114 and 132 days; revisit 6 days in 2017–2021 and 12 days in 2016 and 2022–2024). When combined with a segmentation technique, the index allows for accurate quantification and tracking of temporal changes in lake surface area, highlighting lakes that experienced severe drought from 2016 to 2024 in the French Alps. We successfully validated our lake surface area estimates using state-of-the-art lake databases derived from optical sensors.

Abstract (英文): Mountain glaciers are sensitive indicators of climate change and provide essential freshwater resources for downstream ecosystems and human societies. Although glacier area changes have been extensively investigated in the Greater Caucasus, no long-term assessments of regional glacier ice volume reconstruction have been made. This study presents the first comprehensive reconstruction of glacier ice volume changes across the entire Greater Caucasus over the past 65 years, integrating glacier inventories at five observational periods (1960, 1986, 2000, 2014, and 2025) with the automated PalaeoIce reconstruction framework. Total glacier ice volume declined from 88.08 ± 4.5 km3 in 1960 to 42.66 ± 1.1 km3 in 2025, corresponding to an overall loss of more than half of the Caucasus glacier volume (51.6%) since 1960. Ice volume loss accelerated markedly after 2000, with the most rapid decline (27% or −2.45 yr−1) occurring between 2014 and 2025. Spatial patterns in ice volume change show pronounced regional variability. Relative ice loss over the past 65 years was greatest among glaciers in Azerbaijan, which experienced a 98.4% reduction in ice volume. In comparison, glaciers in Georgia and Russia lost 52.6% and 50.9% of their ice volume, respectively, over the same period. Sectoral analysis further reveals contrasting spatial responses across the Greater Caucasus, with the eastern sector experiencing the greatest proportional ice loss of 71.6% over the 65-year study period, followed by the western sector with 60.4% and the central sector with 47.3%. Despite the continuous regional decline in total ice volume, mean reconstructed glacier thickness increased from ~28.2 m in 1960 to ~33.5 m in 2000 before declining to ~17.4 m by 2025 for the entire Greater Caucasus, indicating substantial changes in the regional glacier-size distribution and geometry through time. These findings provide region-wide quantification of long-term glacier ice volume and thickness evolution in the Greater Caucasus and establish an important baseline for assessing glacier response to ongoing climate change, future water availability, natural hazards, and regional cryospheric evolution.

</details>

Detection precision, recall and F1-score were 0.679, 0.684 and 0.682, while Total (detection plus correct health class) precision, recall and F1-score were 0.627, 0.632 and 0.630, respectively. Compared with spectral-only input (RGB plus vegetation indices), the full 10-channel input improved the Detection F1 from 0.675 to 0.682 and the Total F1 from 0.619 to 0.630. The findings contribute to forest pest early warning systems, hotspot identification and forest health monitoring in Mongolia.

Abstract (英文): Urban environments consist of highly heterogeneous surface materials whose physical and radiative properties influence surface energy exchange, heat storage, and urban microclimate conditions. While urban materials have been extensively investigated in the visible, near-infrared, and shortwave-infrared regions, their characterisation in the long-wave infrared (LWIR) domain remains comparatively limited, particularly using airborne hyperspectral imaging. This study assesses the potential of airborne hyperspectral LWIR emissivity data for urban surface-material characterisation and discrimination. Hyperspectral data were acquired over Dessau-Roßlau, Germany, using the Telops Hyper-Cam Airborne Mini. The radiance data were geometrically corrected, mosaicked, atmospherically compensated, and processed using temperature-emissivity separation to derive a 66-band emissivity mosaic. After MNF-based denoising, 11 material endmembers were extracted from a representative urban-industrial subset, including asphalt, concrete, cobblestone, bitumen roofing, metal surfaces, solar panels, storage silos, tree cover, and sparse grass. Selected airborne endmembers showed generally consistent spectral shapes when compared with independent ECOSTRESS and LUMA laboratory reference spectra. Linear and radial basis function Support Vector Machine classifications were then applied and evaluated using spatially independent validation regions. The linear SVM achieved the highest overall accuracy of 84.95%, balanced accuracy of 85.30%, macro-averaged F1 score of 82.34%, and a kappa coefficient of 0.83, while the RBF-SVM reached 81.19%, 83.06%, 77.68%, and 0.79, respectively. Solar panels, concrete, cobblestone, metallic materials, tree cover, and sparse grass showed high class-specific performance, whereas aged asphalt, bitumen roofing, and storage silos remained more difficult to distinguish. Overall, the results demonstrate the strong potential of airborne hyperspectral LWIR emissivity imaging for urban material discrimination under real-world conditions and support the development of dedicated LWIR spectral libraries for urban environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Comprehending the spatiotemporal dynamics, spatial heterogeneity, and pattern-process relationships of small and micro wetlands (SMWs) holds paramount significance for their conservation, restoration, and sustainable management. Random forest classification, kernel density estimation, standard deviation ellipses, centroid migration, landscape metrics, land-use transition analysis, ecosystem service value (ESV) assessment, and Geodetector were used to characterize SMW change. Total SMW area increased from 53,843.67 ha in 1990 to 71,637.39 ha in 2020, while patch number increased from 104,011 to 125,971; patches smaller than 3 hm2 remained dominant. High-density areas were concentrated in the Jianghuai Plain, Yangtze River plain, Chaohu Lake region, and agricultural drainage networks, with a persistent northeast–southwest distribution orientation. Land-use transitions were dominated by exchanges among cropland, water bodies, and SMWs. At constant 2020 grain prices, total ESV increased from approximately CNY 3.668 billion in 1990 to CNY 6.840 billion in 2020, with hydrological regulation remaining among the largest service components. Geodetector results showed temporally varying single-factor spatial associations, while interaction patterns suggested potential joint spatial associations among natural and anthropogenic factors. These results support spatially targeted SMW conservation and management in Anhui Province.

Abstract (英文): In building change detection from high-resolution remote sensing imagery, genuine building changes typically appear as local alterations in roof contours and spatial layouts, whereas differences in solar elevation, atmospheric conditions, and sensor calibration introduce cross-temporal appearance drift in unchanged regions. When the two types of difference are jointly encoded in a single feature space, appearance drift tends to dominate the network response. We therefore propose SARE-CD, a dense, pixel-wise, soft-gated heterogeneous mixture-of-experts (MoE) framework that combines scene-level bias correction with functionally distinct expert modeling. At the feature front end, SARE-CD generates affine parameters from the channel statistics of the bitemporal change representation and applies them in residual form to suppress systematic radiometric drift. The Structure, Appearance, and Adaptive Experts then model geometric boundary changes, local appearance perturbations, and composite differences, respectively, and a pixel-wise router assigns expert weights at each location, with routing-entropy and weak load-balancing regularization used to stabilize the division of labor among the experts. On LEVIR-CD+, S2Looking, and SYSU-CD, SARE-CD achieves F1 scores of 91.58%, 65.78%, and 84.46%, all of which are the highest among the compared methods. In addition, controlled perturbation experiments show that F1 retention remains high under hue shifts, soft shadows, and small misregistration, whereas extreme additive brightness offsets lead to a notable F1 decline on S2Looking.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Appearance differences between acquisition dates can increase change responses at unchanged locations. Standard supervision on augmented pairs does not explicitly isolate this additional response. We define an input-level counterfactual response as the logit difference produced by the same fixed EMA teacher at the same pixel when the original time-2 image is replaced by a specified appearance intervention. This paired model response holds the scene geometry, label, teacher parameters, and operator realization fixed; it is not a causal reconstruction of the physical acquisition process. We propose counterfactual false-positive mining with reference prediction preservation (CFPM-RP). An exponential-moving-average (EMA) teacher compares an original pair with a pair undergoing a controlled appearance transformation. Positive logit increments within stable unchanged regions define candidate locations. A smooth one-sided loss penalizes the perturbed student response relative to the original teacher prediction, with auxiliary support weighting, segmentation supervision, and changed-region constraints. The auxiliary paths are used only during training, retaining the deployed detector architecture. Experiments cover LEVIR-CD, WHU-CD, and SYSU-CD, eight appearance perturbations, an equal-budget continuation, eight additional comparison methods, and a second backbone. CFPM-RP attains perturbation-averaged F1 values of 91.444%, 71.181%, and 77.842% on the three datasets, respectively. Relative to Generic-FT, false-positive counts decrease by 10.37%, 11.15%, and 11.27%. The results show that CFPM-RP reduces appearance-induced false positives while retaining competitive change-detection performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban bicycle infrastructure requires spatially continuous and quantitative methods for assessing lane width under real operating conditions. This study presents a geo-referenced mobile RGB-D sensing framework for automated bicycle lane width measurement using a lightweight three-wheeled survey platform equipped with a stereo depth camera, GPS receiver, and inertial measurement unit. Bicycle lane boundaries are detected using FastSAM-based segmentation, GPU-accelerated Canny edge detection, and the Hough transform. IMU-assisted depth-to-height conversion distinguishes the road surface from curbs, roadside structures, and obstacles, while temporal-continuity-based lane restoration compensates for faded markings and occlusions. Lane width is estimated from three-dimensional Euclidean distances derived from RGB-D data. The measurements are temporally synchronized with GPS observations and mapped in a geographic information system to visualize spatial width variations along survey routes. Field experiments over four sections totaling 2.87 km showed that 97.79% of the width estimates were within ±5% of the reference width, with a mean absolute error of 0.0218 m. The resulting geo-referenced width maps enable spatially explicit identification of narrow or irregular bicycle lane sections for infrastructure inspection and maintenance.

Abstract (英文): With the rapid expansion of UAV applications, UAVs have posed growing challenges to public safety and airspace management. Pan–tilt–zoom (PTZ) cameras are widely used for monitoring, but conventional rule-based control lacks adaptability in dynamic environments, and existing deep reinforcement learning (DRL) methods mostly rely on discrete action spaces with fixed time steps, preventing fine-grained camera adjustment. Meanwhile, infrared UAVs are often small, distant, and low-resolution, leading to low detection confidence and difficult classification. To address these issues, this paper proposes a hybrid-action-space DRL framework for PTZ camera control. PTZ control is modeled as a discrete-continuous hierarchical subspace: the agent selects a discrete action type and predicts its continuous execution duration for adaptive precise control. A target stable tracking reward function integrating object recognition accuracy, position, and size guides the agent to improve detection confidence and complete recognition efficiently. Furthermore, a lightweight multi-scale feature fusion recognition network combining CNN and Transformer is designed for accurate state representation, and two infrared UAV datasets are constructed to support training and evaluation. Experiments in a Unity3D-based simulation environment show that the proposed framework significantly improves detection confidence and recognition accuracy, maintains high tracking stability across multiple scenarios, and generalizes well with different object detectors, outperforming rule-based and single-action-space baselines.

Abstract (英文): Geostationary infrared observations provide frequent, spatially continuous information on rapidly evolving convective systems. This study assimilates clear-sky and all-sky Channel 10 brightness temperatures (BTs) from GOES-R Advanced Baseline Imager (ABI). The impacts on the analysis and short-range forecast of a continental meso-scale convective system (MCS) are compared. The implementation is achieved in a convection-permitting model using a 5 min cycling ensemble Kalman filter (EnKF) framework. A quality control procedure is developed to enable the effective assimilation of clear-sky ABI observations. Results show that clear-sky assimilation partially removes spurious background clouds and adjusts temperature and moisture in the near-storm environment. All-sky assimilation substantially improves the analyzed cloud field, produces stronger in-storm thermodynamic and dynamical increments, as well as directly constrains hydrometeor variables. In addition, assimilating all-sky observations reduces errors of analyzed surface temperature and humidity by over 25%. Forecast verification further indicates that all-sky assimilation provides more persistent forecast improvements in terms of BT and reflectivity quantities than clear-sky assimilation does. Both clear-sky and all-sky assimilations improve the spatial coverage and intensity of accumulated precipitation, but assimilating all-sky observations produces substantially larger FSS gains (0.163–0.380 versus 0.02–0.13).

In this study, we quantify auroral contamination in RO-based sporadic-E detection and evaluate the performance of both threshold-based and multivariate classifier approaches. Events were labeled as sporadic-E, auroral-E, or non-event from manual ionogram inspection. Two commonly used RO detection methods were applied: an excess phase perturbation Δϕ approach and an amplitude-based S4 approach. Results show that true sporadic-E events are primarily identified when both amplitude- and phase-based thresholds are satisfied. In contrast, more than 80% of auroral-E events satisfy at least one sporadic-E detection criterion, with false triggering of auroral-E and non-events dominated by the phase-based method. A Random Forest classifier was developed as a proof-of-concept model showing success at classifying sporadic-E and non-events, although ambiguity between auroral-E and sporadic-E persists likely due to overlapping phase-perturbation distributions. A Multiple Phase Screen Model is implemented to provide physical insight into the RO observations, where the vertically thicker auroral-E layers are shown to produce significant phase perturbations but negligible amplitude scintillation. These results demonstrate that RO-based detection methods identify E-region structure rather than formation mechanism, implying that high-latitude sporadic-E occurrence rates derived from phased-based RO observations are likely overestimated without additional context.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Semantic segmentation of multispectral satellite imagery is central to large-scale tropical deforestation monitoring, yet cross-region deployment suffers from severe accuracy degradation due to radiometric, atmospheric, and geographic covariate shifts. We introduce dares (Domain Adaptation via Rényi Entropy for Semantic Segmentation), an unsupervised domain adaptation framework that transfers deforestation-mapping models across biomes without target annotations for training. Its core mechanism performs class-conditional Rényi-2 (α=2) alignment on the deepest feature grid together with decoupled confidence-weighted Rényi-2 entropy minimization on target predictions, stabilized against a degenerate collapse as detailed in Methods. Evaluated on an ecologically critical transfer task from the Brazilian Amazon to the Colombian deforestation arc using Sentinel-2 MSI data, dares attains 64.65% target-test mIoU (78.43% DICE) under moderate radiometric attenuation, numerically outperforming Source-Only (44.24%), ADVENT (50.24%), and CBST (48.92%) while maintaining 76.75% source-domain discriminability. Across a Low→Medium→High stress trajectory, dares follows a non-monotonic path from 75.42%→64.65%→69.54%, a 5.9-point Low-to-High change with a Medium dip and a High rebound. It is not the best at Low, where ADVENT (85.78%) and Source-Only (78.93%) exceed its 75.42%, while its advantage opens from Medium (+14.4 points over ADVENT, +20.4 over Source-Only) at the cost of the lowest source-test mIoU (76.75%). Component analysis indicates that decoupled entropy minimization drives target adaptation (−17.2 points if removed), whereas alignment, anti-collapse floors, and repulsion act as source-stabilizing regularizers rather than target maximizers.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate mapping of medicinal plants is essential for resource inventory and sustainable management. While Unmanned Aerial Vehicle (UAV) hyperspectral sensing provides a powerful monitoring tool, the high-dimensional spectral ambiguities and the black-box nature of conventional global classifiers severely restrict mapping accuracy and interpretability. To overcome this, we developed an interpretable, class-specific framework for fine-scale crop identification. A multi-feature pool was distilled through a hierarchical Successive Projections Algorithm (SPA)–Competitive Adaptive Reweighted Sampling (CARS)–Genetic Algorithm (GA)–Recursive Feature Elimination (RFE) strategy, isolating class-specific diagnostic fingerprints that transition mapping from black-box modeling to an explainable mechanism. The tailored feature subsets were supplied to parallel Random Forest binary classifiers to generate class-specific probability outputs, which were subsequently fed into a Logistic Regression decision-level fusion layer that calibrated prediction probabilities and resolved inter-class conflicts to yield the definitive classification results. Applied in Yunfu, Guangdong, the framework mapped target species (Murraya exotica, Zanthoxylum nitidum, and Melicope pteleifolia) with an overall accuracy of 97.35% (Kappa = 0.97), outperforming conventional benchmark models such as standard multi-class Random Forest, Extreme Gradient Boosting, and spectral angle mapper. These findings demonstrate that coupling class-specific feature distillation with probability-calibrated decision fusion provides a robust, interpretable paradigm for precision agricultural mapping in heterogeneous environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The spatial distribution of river ice types during the freeze-up period provides important information on channel freezing and ice-cover development and serves as a basis for ice-condition monitoring and ice-jam flood risk assessment. However, river ice types differ considerably in spatial scale, spectral response, and surface structure, making their fine-scale identification under complex ice conditions challenging. In this study, nine representative frozen reaches along the Inner Mongolia section of the Yellow River were investigated using UAV multispectral orthomosaics with a spatial resolution of approximately 0.15 m. Four representative river-surface classes—open water, transparent ice, smooth ice, and rough ice—were mapped. Their responses in the visible, red-edge, and near-infrared bands were analyzed together with spectral indices and gray-level cooccurrence matrix (GLCM) texture features to characterize interclass spectral and textural differences. A deep-learning mapping model integrating multispectral and textural information was subsequently developed and evaluated on spatially independent reaches. Permutation importance was also used to quantify the contribution of each remote-sensing feature. The results showed that the four surface classes exhibited distinct spectral and textural characteristics. Transparent ice and open water showed limited spectral differences in several bands, whereas texture features captured differences in their local spatial structures and improved the model’s ability to distinguish spectrally similar classes. The model combining spectral indices and GLCM texture features accurately delineated the spatial distributions of the river ice types. In the spatially independent test reaches, it achieved an overall accuracy of 95.80%, a Macro-F1 score of 94.53%, and a mean intersection over union (mIoU) of 90.14%. The model achieved an mIoU of 84.55% on an additional independent test reach with complex ice conditions, which was excluded from training and parameter optimization. Feature-contribution analysis identified spectral indices and texture features as important sources of discriminatory information. NDUWI and NDFSI, together with texture measures calculated from the red and near-infrared bands, ranked among the most informative inputs. This result suggests that the four river-surface classes were distinguished not only by their spectral differences but also by variations in local surface patterns. The UAV imagery therefore supported detailed mapping of heterogeneous frozen reaches and provided spatially explicit information relevant to ice-condition monitoring, analysis of river-ice development, and assessment of potential ice-jam flooding.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large-scale afforestation has been carried out across the Loess Plateau since 1999, yet its impact on terrestrial water storage (TWS) remains uncertain. Here, we integrated GRACE-derived TWS data, leaf area index (LAI), and multiple ancillary datasets, and applied a multivariate regression framework incorporating precipitation, LAI, and temperature to quantify their conditional associations with annual TWS change (TWSC) in the substantially greening region of the Loess Plateau. The signals in the Loess Plateau observed by GRACE were separated into coal mining, soil erosion, leakage of water storage changes from North China, and local TWS using multiple datasets and their variation characteristics were analyzed. In the substantially greening area, annual TWSC was negatively associated with LAI after accounting for precipitation and temperature. Multiplying the fitted LAI coefficient by the observed LAI trend yielded an estimated trend of −2.1 ± 0.5 mm/yr in the LAI-associated component of annual TWSC during the study period. Observed runoff increased by only ~0.6 mm/yr, compared with ~1.7 mm/yr expected from the historical precipitation–runoff relationship, indicating a runoff reduction of ~1.1 ± 0.8 mm/yr relative to that expected from precipitation alone. This departure cannot be uniquely attributed to vegetation because concurrent changes in human activities were not explicitly resolved. Analyses using multiple evapotranspiration products further associated increasing LAI with an ET trend component of approximately 2.0–7.5 mm/yr across the analyzed products. Vegetation greening was associated with regional TWS changes, with enhanced evapotranspiration emerging as an important hydrological pathway. These findings underscore the need to account for non-target mass signals and vegetation–water interactions when evaluating water-cycle responses to ecological restoration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Landslide susceptibility assessment in cold, high-altitude regions is complicated by interactions among topographic, lithological, hydrological, and anthropogenic factors. Machine learning methods are widely used, but model selection, spatially independent evaluation, and interpretation of covariate-adjusted associations remain challenging. This study developed an integrated framework for landslide susceptibility assessment in Tongren City, on the northeastern margin of the Qinghai–Tibet Plateau. The framework combined H2O AutoML, nested spatial cross-validation, Shapley additive explanations (SHAP), partial dependence plots (PDPs), and double machine learning (DML). Each of the 20 sampling realizations included 217 landslide points and 217 non-landslide points, with 13 conditioning factors retained after collinearity screening. DML used partially linear regression with spatial cross-fitting across 200 repetitions to estimate covariate-adjusted associations. Stacked Ensemble achieved the highest mean area under the receiver operating characteristic curve (AUC) of 0.953 ± 0.009 across the 20 realizations. Spatial out-of-fold evaluation showed that the 20% of the study area with the highest susceptibility contained 93.27% ± 1.51% of held-out landslides (mean ± standard deviation). Elevation, distance to roads, lithology, distance to faults, and land use/land cover together accounted for 76.69% of the total mean absolute SHAP values. PDPs revealed nonlinear relationships between conditioning factors and model-predicted susceptibility. Elevation, interpreted as a proxy for valley position, and distance to roads were the only factors with statistically significant negative covariate-adjusted associations after multiple-testing correction. The random common cause (RCC) test indicated that DML estimates remained stable under the random-covariate perturbations examined. The framework distinguishes predictive contributions from covariate-adjusted associations and supports landslide monitoring and spatial planning in cold, high-altitude plateau transition zones.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Bidirectional P3–P4 fusion continuously connects high-resolution features with semantic information from the adjacent level, providing cross-level information for small object detection. The channel kernels in the two directions adapt to their respective inputs, while this feature connection also provides a basis for cooperative learning. We propose controlled reverse-order gradient exchange to exploit the learning signal from the opposite endpoint while retaining independent kernels. Reverse-order mapping pairs opposite offsets in local channel interactions. Each receiver selects directions compatible with its own gradient and limits the auxiliary contribution relative to that norm. The mechanism operates only during training and leaves the inference path unchanged. On VisDrone2019-DET with RT-DETR-R18, reverse-order exchange yields mean gains of 0.419 mAP50 and 0.254 mAP50–95 percentage points over independent kernels across three training seeds. Mean small-object AP increases by 0.323 points. At confidence 0.25, prediction matching yields 105.3 additional correct matches on average. These results suggest that controlled cooperation between independent channel kernels can benefit small object detection.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The F2-layer peak electron density (NmF2), the F2-layer peak height (hmF2), and the α-Chapman scale height (H) are key parameters for characterizing the vertical structure of ionospheric electron density, and of great significance for space environment monitoring and space weather applications. Using Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) radio occultation (GRO) observations from the CSES-01 satellite, this study proposes the CSES-NET model. A reliable sample data set is first constructed through sample screening, α-Chapman fitting, and physical constraints. On this basis, a multitask model integrating convolutional neural network (CNN), long short-term memory (LSTM) network, and an attention mechanism is developed to simulate the three parameters. The results show that the proposed model reproduces the spatiotemporal characteristics of NmF2, hmF2, and H well and effectively improves the reconstruction accuracy of electron density profiles (EDP). Baseline and ablation experiments are also carried out to ascertain the optimal modeling scheme. This study may provide a reference for reconstructing ionospheric profiles from the key parameters of the Chapman profile under quiet geomagnetic conditions.

Abstract (英文): Polarimetric synthetic aperture radar (PolSAR) image classification requires the joint use of polarimetric scattering information and spatial context. However, aggregating or propagating information across locations with different scattering characteristics may mix features from dissimilar regions, and recurrent state propagation in Mamba can extend this influence along scan paths. Class-agnostic regions generated by the Segment Anything Model (SAM) from Pauli pseudo-RGB images may also span multiple land-cover classes, which may reduce the reliability of direct regional consistency constraints. This study proposes SSHMamba, a SAM-assisted scattering-guided hybrid selective Mamba network. It constructs a seven-channel scattering prior from the Pauli components, span, and H/A/α parameters. The local branch combines center-outward spiral rearrangement, one-dimensional convolution, and neighborhood weighting based on spatial distance and scattering similarity. In the long-range branch, the scattering prior conditions the state-space parameters, while scattering similarity and a learnable gate regulate the contribution of preceding states. Multidirectional outputs are aggregated using pixel-wise direction weights, and local and long-range features are fused using scattering-prior-driven channel-wise coefficients. During training, SAM candidate regions are screened using scattering homogeneity, prediction reliability, and region area before a soft regional consistency constraint is applied; SAM is not used during inference. Across three independent training runs under the same fixed data split, SSHMamba achieves 99.12 ± 0.04% OA, 99.13 ± 0.04% AA, and 0.9904 ± 0.0005 Kappa on Flevoland, and 99.42 ± 0.02% OA, 97.52 ± 0.03% AA, and 0.9909 ± 0.0004 Kappa on San Francisco. In the fixed reference runs, ECP-Mamba yields a slightly higher OA on Flevoland (99.30% versus 99.16%), whereas SSHMamba yields a higher OA on San Francisco (99.44% versus 99.20%). On Flevoland, removing the complete scattering-guided selective state propagation mechanism reduces OA from 99.16% to 96.48%. The SAM ablation shows that direct consistency over raw SAM regions yields 97.12% OA, candidate-region reliability screening increases it to 98.72%, and the full reliability-controlled SAM-assisted strategy reaches 99.16%. On the DLR E-SAR Oberpfaffenhofen dataset, SSHMamba achieves 99.29 ± 0.07% OA, 99.15 ± 0.04% AA, and 0.9889 ± 0.0006 Kappa across three runs, providing additional cross-sensor and cross-scene evidence.

</details>

Abstract (英文): SAR tomography (TomoSAR) is capable of separating scatterers at different elevations within the same range–azimuth resolution cell. Differential TomoSAR (D-TomoSAR) further incorporates temporal information, enabling joint estimation of scatterer elevation and deformation velocity. Most existing sparse inversion methods rely on single-polarization observations, and the joint use of observations across polarization channels remains limited. Separating adjacent and weak scatterers in complex urban layover scenarios remains challenging. Sparse Bayesian learning (SBL) offers strong capabilities for sparse support recovery and super-resolution parameter estimation. When SBL is applied over a global fine grid in the two-dimensional elevation–deformation parameter space, the high correlation among neighboring dictionary atoms may hinder the separation of adjacent scatterers and affect the stability of sparse support recovery. To address these issues, this paper proposes a two-step sparse Bayesian learning (TSSBL) D-TomoSAR method with a dual-polarization shared-support constraint. First, joint Orthogonal Matching Pursuit (OMP) search is performed on a coarse grid using HH/VV observations to achieve global coarse localization of potential scatterers. Subsequently, a local fine grid is constructed around the candidate positions, and SBL is performed under an HH/VV shared-support constraint to achieve refined estimation of scatterer elevation and linear deformation velocity. Finally, the number of effective scatterers is determined through a statistical test based on residual reduction. Simulation and real-data experiments demonstrate favorable scatterer separation and joint elevation–deformation estimation performance. Using 22 PAZ HH/VV images, the proposed Dual-Pol TSSBL method achieves a building-height root-mean-square error (RMSE) of 2.72 m for five buildings with reference heights. The results indicate that combining dual-polarization shared support with coarse-to-fine SBL inversion can effectively improve joint elevation–deformation estimation for urban buildings in D-TomoSAR under limited observations, with potential for future application to large-scale urban building deformation monitoring using long SAR time series.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Rapid cataloging of non-cooperative targets in newly formed space debris clouds requires robust radar-based multi-target tracking to form valid observation arcs and reliable association of arcs acquired during temporally separated observation windows. Dense target distributions and ambiguous measurement-to-track and arc-to-arc relationships often result in missed measurements and false associations. To address the challenge, this study proposes an iterative joint probabilistic data association (JPDA) procedure to perform radar tracking of multiple targets in a single observation window, in which a data mining operation is implemented after each JPDA iteration to maximize observation utilization rate. In the two-arc association stage, candidate arc pairs are first constructed using two-arc association algorithm based on the Lambert boundary-value problem, and are then further screened according to the residuals of two-arc orbit determination. This strategy suppresses false associations while maintaining high true positive associations. The algorithms were experimented with simulated radar observations of 100 targets in the Iridium 33-Cosmos 2251 debris cloud in the time period 2 h–4 h after the collision. Results show that the iterative JPDA tracking increased the number of successfully tracked targets in the first observation window to 96 from 88 using the single-run JPDA tracking, corresponding to an improvement of approximately 9%. The first-round data-mining operation increased the minimum observation utilization rate from 54.58% to 71.67%, demonstrating its effectiveness in recovering unused observations in the JPDA tracking. For the two-arc association, the Lambert-problem-based method suffered a false association rate of 95.17%, although it achieved an association recall rate of 96.88%. In contrast, the improved two-arc association method reduced the false association rate to 5.26% and maintained a high association recall rate of 93.75%. These results demonstrate that the proposed framework supports rapid and reliable tracking, association, orbit determination, and cataloging of non-cooperative space targets.

At a 0.1 mm/day threshold, CSI = 0.653 and HSS = 0.720 exceeded the two specified ablations. Grouped RMSE favored the ablations, whereas grouped MAE differences were not robust. Restricting MaxPrec to 1961–2018 retained better MAE, RMSE, and KGE than all five original products. However, CSI/HSS fell to 0.633/0.698, approximately matching GSMaP-Gauge in CSI but falling below its HSS. The event-skill advantage over the ablation without MaxPrec nearly disappeared, indicating strong dependence on the reference period. Full-sample evaluation included both training and temporal-evaluation periods and yielded pooled MAE = 0.677 mm/day and KGE = 0.783. Of the 2,004,598 full-sample records, 1,647,464 (82.2%) were from model development. GTCH provided an assumption-limited relative-uncertainty diagnostic; the lower minimum statistic after candidate-set expansion was interpreted descriptively, without establishing true uncertainty reduction. Abstract (英文): Accurate spectral and radiometric calibration is essential for reliable greenhouse gas retrieval from satellite hyperspectral observations. However, long-term in-orbit operation may introduce wavelength shifts and radiometric response inconsistencies, particularly within narrow atmospheric absorption bands, reducing the reliability of Level-1 radiance products. This study proposes an observation-dependent adaptive reference spectrum framework for spectral and radiometric calibration of the Greenhouse Gas Monitoring Instrument (GMI) onboard the GaoFen-5B (GF-5B) satellite. The proposed framework integrates the SCIATRAN radiative transfer model with the instrument line shape (ILS) of GMI to generate physically consistent reference spectra under varying observation conditions. Sensitivity analysis was performed to identify dominant parameters affecting simulated radiance spectra, and an adaptive reference spectrum library was constructed by considering surface reflectance, solar zenith angle, aerosol conditions, and geographic characteristics. The generated reference spectra were subsequently used to estimate spectral shifts and correct radiometric response deviations between observed and simulated spectra. GF-5B GMI observations acquired from September 2021 to December 2024 over Dunhuang, Golmud, and Railroad Valley (RRV) were used for validation. Results demonstrate that the proposed framework effectively reduces spectral offsets, improves radiometric consistency, and enhances spectral agreement for the O2, CO2-1, and CO2-2 bands. The multi-site validation confirms the robustness of the proposed approach under different surface and atmospheric conditions. This study provides a physically constrained and transferable calibration framework for improving the long-term spectral and radiometric consistency of greenhouse gas hyperspectral observations and supporting more reliable atmospheric composition retrievals.

</details>
