Abstract (英文): Global climate change increases the frequency of synoptic forcing, across the Mediterranean Basin. These systems often trigger convective storms characterized by intense rainfall, resulting in the formation of linear erosion features (LEFs), such as gullies. Previous studies focused on the morphological characteristics of LEFs, whereas the relationships among LEF geomorphology, landscape topography, and rainfall were poorly studied. This knowledge gap is attributable to the multitude of topographic and rainfall variables, which make manual analysis both challenging and time-consuming. Recent advances in high-spatial-resolution satellite imagery, together with machine learning techniques, provide a potential alternative. Accordingly, the following objectives were defined: (1) design a framework for quantifying temporal changes in fresh herbaceous vegetation across different natural and human-induced linear erosion features (LEFs) in arid hilly loess landscapes using satellite imagery data; (2) develop a predictive model for geomorphological patterns of LEFs based on rainfall and topographic parameters. Three sites were studied, all in the northern Negev, with a semiarid climate and loess deposits covered with a dense LEF net. A unique framework was developed, including the use of acquired PlanetScope® imagery datasets, the allocation of elevation segments along the LEFs, and the normalization of the median NDVI value of each segment by that of the reference plot. Using ‘Random Forest’, the importance, and the directionality of the factors affecting the LEF fresh herbaceous over the elevation segments was determined by a linear correlation. The finding indicates that the NFVR of the LEF baseline (Intercept) was affected only by <1% from the aspect, while there was a 26% increase in NFVR downslope. The LEF (or segment) length affects the Intercept of the NFVR correlation, changing by 21% and 37%, respectively. The steepness affects the NFVR Intercept only by (0–8%), while there was a 43% effect on the NFVR change. In practice, the framework enables the prediction of segments vulnerable to land collapse, and adequate management of gullied areas.

Abstract (英文): Seabed pockmarks are important geomorphological indicators of shallow fluid migration, shallow gas activity, and gas hydrate-related processes. However, automatic pockmark detection from shaded-relief bathymetric imagery remains challenging because many pockmarks have small image-space footprints, occur densely, have weak boundaries, and are embedded in complex seabed backgrounds. To address these issues, this study proposes FSLA-RTDETR, an improved four-scale RT-DETR network for dense small-object seabed pockmark detection. The model introduces SAC-C2f to enhance high-level feature representation, the LPEA Encoder to improve global contextual modeling, and a DGCST-based bidirectional feature fusion network to integrate shallow spatial details and deep semantic information. In addition, the high-resolution P2 feature is incorporated into the decoder to improve small-object localization. A pockmark detection dataset was constructed from publicly available BOEM bathymetric data from the northern Gulf of Mexico, containing 880 images and 8013 annotated pockmark instances. Experimental results show that FSLA-RTDETR achieves mAP50, mAP75, and mAP50–95 values of 0.9218, 0.6221, and 0.5485, respectively. Compared with the baseline RT-DETR, mAP75 and mAP50–95 increase by 6.01 and 2.22 percentage points, respectively. Under the present no-pretraining setting, comparative experiments further demonstrate that FSLA-RTDETR achieves superior high-IoU localization performance compared with the representative object detection models evaluated in this study, providing an effective method for automatic seabed pockmark detection and geomorphological mapping.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The erosion of coastal chalk cliffs is a significant concern for communities in Northern Europe, with rates of retreat varying widely depending on local conditions. This study presents six years of UAV photogrammetry monitoring of chalk cliffs in Sussex, UK, producing 2.5D change detection maps to quantify erosion at Telscombe and Peacehaven. The results demonstrate a strong correlation between rates of lower-cliff erosion and wave energy at Peacehaven, with an r2 value of 0.82. However, periods of intensive mass-wasting can render these relationships unreliable. The relationship for Telscombe, which experiences much more mass-wasting, demonstrates an r2 of 0.37, which improves to 0.48 with the removal of an outlier. Cumulative erosion patterns follow a gradualistic trend, superimposed by episodic mass-wasting, with lower-cliff erosion rates increasing by approximately 3.2 times during storm-forced high-energy intervals compared to stable periods. A similar pattern is seen for mass-wasting events, although erosion intensities may be more than 6 times greater during periods of storminess. These findings highlight the role of storminess in accelerating erosion in the coastal zone.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Although deep learning-based approaches have significantly advanced intelligent radar image interpretation, existing methods predominantly rely on single-view inputs, which inherently limits target recognition performance. In practical scenarios, multi-view radar imagery provides richer structural and feature representations of targets compared to single-view observations. To effectively exploit this comprehensive information, we propose a new Multi-view Laplacian Propagation Fusion Network (MLPFN) for radar image target recognition. First, a Reparameterization Perspective-Aware (RPA) backbone is designed, incorporating a Dual-level Multi-path Rep-Kernel (DMPK) and a Multi-level Dual-path Attention Module (MDAM). This architecture mitigates the sensitivity of recognition performance to aspect angle variations and enhances the extraction of discriminative target features. Second, a Laplacian Multi-view Feature Propagation Algorithm (LMFPA) is introduced to encode structural correlations among different views, thereby augmenting the feature discriminability for multi-view targets. Third, a Multi-view Feature Adaptive Fusion Module (MFAFM) is developed to integrate global contextual information across views into a unified representation, reducing redundancy while maximizing feature distinctiveness. Furthermore, a Stage-wise Guided Constraint Training Mechanism (SGCTM) is devised to supervise the feature extraction process across different network layers during training, refining the network’s feature learning and recognition capabilities. Extensive experiments conducted on multi-view radar image datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed MLPFN.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monitoring the dynamics of gullies in Mediterranean agrosystems is critical but severely limited by agricultural practices that generate continuous machinery compaction and tillage of the soil, camouflaging topographic features and disturbing erosion analyses. This study discusses strategies to quantify erosion by distinguishing natural erosion from agricultural disturbance, integrating multitemporal LiDAR data (2014, 2020, 2024) with gully inventories using orthophoto maps (2011–2022). The volumetric changes were assessed using DoD for the periods 2014–2020 and 2020–2024, and the morphometric performance of the TNO index was further validated using ROC curves in R. Both approaches performed consistently higher in detecting permanent gullies. The TNO index has proven its usefulness for semi-automated delimitation in large or low-data areas. By intersecting DoD surfaces with the gully inventory, localized erosion processes were successfully isolated from the surrounding agricultural landscape matrix. The quantified rate of erosion in the gullies refers strictly to their internal dynamics, which have been close to the natural threshold for soil formation, indicating a risk of soil degradation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Mountain forest greenness varies across seasons, yet growing-season averages can mask season-specific relationships with climate variability. We assessed seasonal and elevational variation in historical forest greenness within areas classified as forest in 2024, temporal variation in spring NDVI–climate relationships, and the association between nighttime light (NTL) intensity and forest greenness in Changbai Mountain from 2000 to 2024. We used MODIS-derived normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI), 1 km temperature and precipitation datasets, elevation zones, and annual nighttime light (NTL) data. We applied a forest mask derived from the 2024 Annual China Land Cover Dataset (CLCD) and retained 1 km pixels with at least 70% forest cover. NDVI increased significantly over the aggregate growing season (slope = 0.0011 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.584, p < 0.001), summer (slope = 0.0006 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.188, p = 0.030), and autumn (slope = 0.0019 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.280, p = 0.007). Autumn showed the largest estimated slope; this ranking alone does not establish statistical differences among seasonal slopes. Spring showed no significant long-term trend (slope = 0.0011 NDVI yr−1, R2 = 0.087, p = 0.152), but spring NDVI showed a strong positive correlation with temperature at the regional scale (r = 0.760, p < 0.001), with positive correlations maintained across all elevation zones. In spring regression models, temperature showed a larger standardized coefficient magnitude than precipitation across elevation zones, with all variance inflation factors ≤ 1.22. Exploratory five-year sliding-window correlations suggested temporal variation in spring NDVI–climate relationships, although each estimate was based on only five annual observations. At low elevation, the NTL–NDVI regression was significant (R2 = 0.198, p = 0.026), whereas associations at middle and high elevations were not statistically significant; this raw association should be interpreted cautiously because both variables may contain concurrent temporal trends. These results describe seasonal and elevational greenness patterns within the 2024 forest mask, highlighting the distinct spring temperature association and elevation-dependent relationships between NTL intensity and forest greenness.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Timely and accurate mapping of flood inundation extents in rice-growing regions is essential for flood damage assessment. However, fragmented fields and heterogeneous land cover in complex terrain continue to pose challenges. This study proposes a two-stage mapping framework that integrates multi-resolution remote sensing imagery with deep-learning-based semantic segmentation. First, a dual-attention and edge-aware attention network (DAEA-Net) was developed to derive a high-accuracy paddy rice map from very high-resolution (VHR) imagery. Second, the flood extent in paddy fields was delineated using the prior-histogram marker-controlled watershed (PH-MCW) method on SAR imagery with area-of-interest (AOI) filtering based on terrain slope and on the permanent water bodies and potentially inundated land types of the land-type survey data. On the test set, DAEA-Net achieved the highest OA, IoU, F1-score, and Kappa among the compared semantic segmentation models, including U-Net, HRNet, and DeepLabV3+, with values of 0.9205, 0.8747, 0.9331, and 0.8352, respectively. PH-MCW segmentation with AOI filtering, which removes terrain-induced false alarms while retaining water surfaces, improved flood-water detection, achieving F1-scores of 0.821 and 0.764 on two flood-validation reference datasets (NDRCC and AIR-PolSAR-Seg-2.0), respectively. The proposed framework provides a semi-automated solution for mapping flood inundation over paddy rice fields in complex terrain, in which the prior water proportion is refined by local inspection before application.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Marine gravity is traditionally derived from altimeter data using physics-based methods, which are sensitive to altimeter data noise. Although deep learning methods can capture nonlinear relationships between marine gravity and multi-source data, purely data-driven approaches depend strongly on the quality and spatial distribution of training ship-borne gravity data. To combine the advantages of both methods, this study proposes a hybrid marine gravity recovery method that integrates the Inverse Vening Meinesz (IVM) method into a convolutional neural network (CNN) framework. Specifically, the IVM-derived marine gravity is incorporated into the CNN loss function as a constraint, and distance- and topography-dependent hyperparameters are introduced to control its contribution during training. The gravity model derived by the proposed IVM-constrained CNN method is validated using independent ship-borne gravity observations and a recognized marine gravity model. Assessed by ship-borne data, the proposed method improves accuracy by 28.5% and 6.8% compared with the IVM-derived and CNN-derived gravity models, respectively. Comparisons with the recognized model also indicate that the gravity model derived by the proposed method achieves the highest accuracy among the three models. These results show that embedding IVM constraints into deep learning frameworks can significantly improve the accuracy and robustness of marine gravity recovery.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Lake water color provides an integrated indicator of aquatic environmental change, yet its long-term phenology and resilience remain poorly understood in arid regions. In this study, Landsat-derived hue angle (α) records were reconstructed for 350 lakes larger than 1 km2 across arid Northwest China from 1984 to 2025. Regional mean α declined significantly over the past 42 years (R2 = 0.51), accompanied by pronounced spatial heterogeneity. Three lake water color phenological types were identified: Aseasonal (75.0%), Summer Greening (13.7%), and Spring Greening (11.3%). Aseasonal lakes were the most stable, whereas seasonal lakes showed greater temporal variability and a tendency to transition toward aseasonality. Lake water color resilience increased in 43% of lakes, declined in 21%, and remained unchanged in 36%, with resilience-loss lakes showing distinct spatial clustering. Lake morphology was most strongly associated with water color change, topography and thermal conditions with its phenology, and watershed population dynamics with its resilience. In situ observations further showed significant relationships between α and optically active constituents and their derived parameters, reflecting the combined optical effects of absorption and scattering and suggesting its potential as a proxy for broader water-quality assessment with more extensive field sampling and validation. These findings highlight contrasting controls on lake-color dynamics, phenology, and resilience, and demonstrate the value of hue angle for monitoring environmental change in vulnerable arid-zone lakes. Overall, these results advance understanding of the long-term environmental responses of arid-zone lakes and provide a scientific basis for targeted monitoring and adaptive management in arid regions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vendor-provided rational polynomial coefficients (RPCs) for high-resolution optical satellite imagery often contain systematic biases that are commonly corrected using field-surveyed ground control points (GCPs). Acquiring such points can be costly or infeasible in inaccessible regions. This study proposes an RPC bias compensation framework for KOMPSAT-3 (K3) optical imagery based on the geometric accuracy and stability of KOMPSAT-5 (K5) X-band SAR enhanced high-resolution (EH) imagery, without measuring image coordinates of control points. Three-dimensional ground control data (GCD) are projected into both sensor image spaces via the K5 Range–Doppler (RD) model and the K3 RPC model. A GCD-driven projective transformation provides coarse alignment, followed by fine registration using FFT-based phase correlation (FPC). Registration-derived correspondence coordinates are then used to estimate a strongly regularized image-space affine bias model, yielding a predominantly translational correction. Three GCD sources—Korean public control points (PCPs), ICESat-2 altimetry, and the 30 m SRTM DEM—were evaluated using one K3–K5 image pair over Suncheon, Korea. A sensitivity analysis showed that control points on flat terrain (elevation ≤60 m, slope ≤6°) yielded stable results, whereas points on steep or forested terrain degraded the registration, and increasing the number of GCD points did not systematically improve accuracy. Under these conditions, all three sources reduced the RMSE at independent GNSS checkpoints from 7.1 and 10.6 pixels in the column and row directions to 1.6–2.0 and 1.6–2.1 pixels, respectively. This accuracy is comparable to that of conventional GCP-based compensation (1.7/1.3 pixels), whereas two structural-feature-based matching methods (HOPC and CFOG) did not improve on the original RPCs under the tested implementations. These results demonstrate the potential of SRTM-derived control data for meter-level directional refinement of optical imagery when reliable SAR–optical registration is achieved. Generalization beyond the tested image pair requires further validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Weakly supervised remote sensing change detection reduces annotation costs but suffers from incomplete class activation maps, unreliable pseudo-label propagation, and insufficient discrimination between adjacent change regions. To address these limitations, we propose PRIWCD, a weakly supervised framework for remote sensing change detection. PRIWCD introduces a dynamic prototype comparison module to enhance high-level differential features by modeling unchanged regions and diverse change patterns with multiple dynamic prototypes. A dual relationship propagation guidance module further refines class activation maps by jointly exploiting changes in bi-temporal attention relationships and feature similarity. In addition, instance separation regularization improves feature compactness within pseudo-instances and enhances the separation between changed regions and the background. Experiments on WHU-CD, CLCD, and DSIFN demonstrate the effectiveness of PRIWCD. The proposed method achieves the best mean F1 scores of 79.10%, 62.33%, and 62.68%, respectively, outperforming the compared weakly supervised methods. The results demonstrate that PRIWCD effectively improves pseudo-label quality and provides robust change detection under image-level supervision.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To address the challenge of accurately inverting cloud base height (CBH) from passive remote sensing, this study proposes Hetero-CBHNet, a heterogeneous stacked integrated network framework for CBH inversion. The framework uses random forest (RF), convolutional neural network (CNN), and Transformer models as a pool of candidate base models and employs the RidgeCV algorithm to automatically learn the fusion weights. A total of 1,011,620 samples from FY-4A and MODIS, covering the period from May 2019 to May 2020, were selected (with CloudSat/CALIPSO 2B-CLDCLASS-LIDAR as the ground truth), and the dataset was split chronologically. Across the two data sources under the chronological splitting protocol, Hetero-CBHNet achieves RMSE values of 2966.12 m and 2797.64 m and R values of 0.630 and 0.659 on the test sets, outperforming the individual models. Feature analysis shows that incorporating operational passive cloud type products does not yield a stable gain, indicating that current passive cloud type information is not yet sufficient to replace active satellite labels. In addition, the RMSE values under the random splitting protocol are 1510.01 m and 1488.41 m, substantially better than those under chronological splitting, which confirms the strong dependence of the conclusions on the evaluation protocol.

Abstract (英文): Long-term satellite precipitable water vapor (PWV) records are widely used in climate studies, but excellent monthly agreement does not necessarily imply equivalent long-term climate-record consistency. We evaluated Atmospheric Infrared Sounder (AIRS) Version 7 Level-3 monthly PWV against Integrated Global Radiosonde Archive Version 2 (IGRA2) observations at 76 Chinese stations during 2005–2025, using 19,053 matched station-months. The overall agreement was high (R = 0.984, bias = −1.34 mm, RMSE = 3.36 mm). Nevertheless, station-by-calendar-month leave-one-year-out anomalies showed a robust moisture-state dependence of AIRS–IGRA differences (Spearman ρ = −0.722; 95% CI −0.741 to −0.704). The AIRS-on-IGRA OLS anomaly-response slope was 0.473; equal-variance Deming regression increased it to 0.560 (95% CI 0.519–0.596), still below unity. The ascending-minus-descending monthly PWV averaged +0.561 mm and was largest in summer. The network annual bias became more negative at −0.141 mm yr−1, compared with −0.138 mm yr−1 for a strict 73-station fixed network, while the annual R had no significant trend. The station-scale trend consistency was moderate (Pearson r = 0.545; sign agreement 65.8%). An independent BJFS GNSS comparison supported an AIRS contribution to local divergence, but did not establish nationwide attribution. These findings support monthly PWV climatology and broad variability applications over China while showing that small long-term trends require independent constraints, reference-record homogenization, and explicit attention to sampling stability.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Nighttime light (NTL) remote sensing provides a globally consistent means of observation of human activity after dark, but its sustainability meaning is context dependent. This study analyzes annual VIIRS Day/Night Band nighttime-light radiance for 467 urban centers from 2015 to 2023 using the same 2015 Global Human Settlement Layer urban-center boundary for every year. Holding the spatial footprint constant provides an apples-to-apples longitudinal comparison: changes in Sum VNL represent changes in satellite-observed radiance within the same geographic area rather than changes caused by expanding or redefining the urban boundary. Across the 467 fixed urban areas, total Sum VNL increased from 7.43 million in 2015 to 8.51 million in 2023 (+14.49%); 374 cities increased and 93 decreased. The sample declined by 2.49% from 2019 to 2020 and increased by 5.44% from 2020 to 2021. Regional 2015–2023 changes were +27.96% in Asia, +16.17% in Africa, +14.78% in Oceania, +11.69% in Latin America and the Caribbean, +3.73% in Northern America, and −1.96% in Europe. Mean VNL is reported alongside Sum VNL to distinguish average radiance intensity from total radiance within each fixed urban footprint. The results demonstrate substantial spatial and temporal heterogeneity but do not by themselves prove energy poverty, over-illumination, electricity consumption, or health exposure. NTL is therefore interpreted as a complementary diagnostic variable for SDGs 3, 7, and 11 that requires contextual validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): High-resolution multi-timescale SPEI data are important for drought monitoring and climate-impact studies, but long-term high-resolution datasets remain limited over large regions. Monthly labels were extracted from daily records at 427 stations using an exact source-calendar month-end rule. Monthly 0.1° CMFD predictors were mapped to stations and 1 km pixel centres using a NaN-aware bilinear sampler and combined with complete-window temporal features and NASADEM terrain covariates. Five timescale-specific LightGBM models generated 2359 nationwide monthly rasters. Random five-fold cross-validation yielded mean R2 values of 0.8807–0.9122; 200 km spatial-block validation with a 100 km exclusion buffer yielded 0.7857–0.8739; and forward blocked-period validation yielded 0.7577–0.8337. Full-period product comparison gave Pearson r = 0.9295 against Zhang-SPEI for SPEI-3, while comparison with HSPEI across five timescales yielded r = 0.7772–0.3267. Spatially buffered drought-condition validation produced F1 scores of 0.7021–0.7838 for moderate-or-worse drought, whereas extreme-drought Recall was 0.2397–0.3869. The dataset provides a long-term, multi-timescale resource for drought monitoring and climate-impact research across mainland China, with validation results characterizing its performance across regions, periods, and drought-severity levels.

Abstract (英文): Aerosol optical depth characterizes total aerosol loading in the atmospheric column but does not directly indicate its vertical distribution relative to the planetary boundary layer. This study investigates aerosol vertical structure over Daejeon, Korea, by integrating column aerosol optical properties from a sky radiometer, near-surface atmospheric extinction measurements, and planetary boundary layer height from reanalysis data. Aerosol scale height is derived from aerosol optical depth at 870 nm and near-surface extinction and is analyzed alongside aerosol optical and microphysical properties. Aerosol scale height exhibits substantial variability relative to the planetary boundary layer height, with the highest relative values occurring in spring and the lowest in autumn. An increase in aerosol scale height relative to the boundary layer is accompanied by a decrease in the Ångström exponent and an increase in the coarse-mode volume fraction. The coarse-mode enhancement is driven mainly by an increase in coarse-mode aerosol volume, whereas surface PM10 concentrations show only a weak relationship with variations in vertical structure. Single scattering albedo generally increases, indicating a more scattering dominated column aerosol population. These results demonstrate that combining aerosol scale height with planetary boundary layer height provides valuable information on aerosol vertical structure and the degree of correspondence between surface and column aerosol conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sea ice detection and classification are critical for navigation safety and climate change research. With its high-resolution, all-weather, and day-and-night imaging capabilities, Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) has become a vital data source for these tasks. However, most existing deep learning studies rely on amplitude data, failing to fully exploit complex-valued information. This study evaluates whether locally filtered inter-channel phase information from Sentinel-1 Single Look Complex (SLC) data provides complementary information to conventional HH and HV amplitudes. Using the AI4 Arctic Sea Ice Challenge Dataset (ASID Challenge), we construct a complex-valued dataset and evaluate different input combinations with four representative semantic segmentation networks. We examine the classification results across six different categories in the ASID Challenge and, in conjunction with real navigation scenarios, map these six categories into three classes: Open Water, Early-stage Ice, and Developed Ice. For FCN under direct three-class training, phase augmentation improved the mean test Macro-F1 and mIoU by 4.4% and 6.1%. The results show that although its benefit is not consistent across all six-class classification experiments, phase information provides more reliable improvements when sea ice types are grouped according to broader development stages. These findings suggest that inter-channel phase contains complementary information beyond amplitude and may improve SAR sea ice classification under practical application scenarios.

Abstract (英文): Spinach (Spinacia oleracea L.) is an economically important leafy vegetable whose nitrogen management remains comparatively understudied with remote sensing tools relative to major row crops. This study evaluated the potential of unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV)-based hyperspectral imagery to estimate foliar nitrogen content (%N) in field-grown spinach at a commercial plot across two growing seasons and four nitrogen fertilization treatments. Nine spectral indices were related to destructively measured %N at four phenological stages of the Biologische Bundesanstalt, Bundessortenamt und CHemische Industrie (BBCH) scale (BBCH 16, 30, 45, and 49) under three modeling scenarios, ranging from flight-specific regression to a continuous model incorporating days after sowing (DAS) as a proxy for crop development, with model performance assessed out-of-sample by repeated cross-validation. Foliar nitrogen content differed significantly among treatments from BBCH 30 onward and only marginally at the earliest stage, with the two highest doses converging into a statistically indistinguishable class. The Vogelmann red-edge index 3 (VOGREI3) yielded the most accurate estimates, peaking at BBCH 45 (cross-validated coefficient of determination, R2 = 0.845) before declining significantly at BBCH 49, a loss consistent with spectral saturation of the closed canopy. A single continuous model incorporating DAS covered the BBCH 45 and BBCH 49 flights with a relative error of 12.4%, against 9.6% and 14.9% for the corresponding flight-specific models, and required no stage-specific recalibration within the calibration seasons. In an unseen season, plot ranking was preserved, and a few reference samples corrected the bias. UAV hyperspectral imagery combined with development-stage-aware modeling therefore shows potential for non-destructive nitrogen monitoring in spinach once locally calibrated, although transferability across further seasons, sites and cultivars remains to be established.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Terrain generalization—the scale-driven abstraction of digital elevation models (DEMs) that retains dominant landforms while filtering trivial microtopography—has long been dominated by rule-based algorithms, whereas deep learning has mainly targeted the inverse task of DEM super-resolution. This study proposes TG-CNN, a feature-fusion convolutional neural network for DEM down-scaling generalization. The network compresses the high-resolution DEM through a max- or average-pooling front end, combines normalized elevation with slope and sine–cosine circularly encoded aspect as complementary input channels, and is trained with a composite elevation–slope–aspect loss. Training, validation, and testing are conducted on geographically independent mountainous areas under a leave-one-area-out scheme, followed by adjustable IDW fusion post-processing. On the test region, classical bilinear interpolation retains the best elevation accuracy, while the standalone TG-CNN does not reach a comparable level; fused accuracy improves monotonically as the IDW share increases, indicating that the fusion benefit originates from the interpolation component. On the ridge–valley structure, however, TG-CNN behaves as an aggressive yet reliable filter: it achieves the highest precision and the smallest positional offset among all compared methods but retains only part of the reference skeleton, leaving its F1-score below the interpolation baseline. TG-CNN thus realizes a genuine, controllable terrain-abstraction effect, yet deep learning does not yet surpass mature interpolation in pixel-level elevation accuracy—a negative result that clarifies current limitations and points toward residual learning and information-preserving architectures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The increasing frequency of freshwater algal blooms threatens aquatic ecosystems globally. While remote sensing algorithms are widely used for bloom detection, conventional methods frequently exhibit degraded performance under optically complex conditions (e.g., sunglint, thin clouds, and high turbidity) and lack sensitivity to sparse blooms. To address these limitations, this study introduces the Red-Edge Floating Algae Index (RFAI). Structured as a baseline-height model, the RFAI mathematically isolates the scattering-induced spectral curvature of algal blooms using three critical Sentinel-2 Multispectral Instrument (MSI) bands: the green reflectance peak (∼560 nm), the red absorption trough (∼665 nm), and the red-edge reflectance peak (∼704 nm). This formulation effectively decouples genuine target signals from confounding background noise. Comparative validation against 17 widely used indices across a diverse set of inland lakes demonstrated the statistically significant overall effectiveness of the RFAI ( p < 0.0001), yielding precision and F1-score improvements of up to 42.8 % and 26.6 %, respectively, under locally optimized parameterizations. Crucially, the RFAI demonstrates robust comprehensive stability across highly variable, optically challenging conditions, effectively mitigating the severe performance fluctuations often observed in individual conventional indices. Supported by a novel optical-type-stratified thresholding strategy, the RFAI establishes a scalable and versatile framework for operational bloom monitoring using compatible red-edge satellite systems, enabling the generation of reliable observational records essential for the sustainable management of aquatic ecosystems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Time-series Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) provides dense displacement observations along urban rail corridors, yet converting point-based measurements into corridor-scale diagnostic information remains challenging. This study proposes the Metro-InSAR Risk Index (MIRI), a diagnostic index designed to convert multi-dimensional InSAR-derived displacement characteristics into displacement-related diagnostic levels for urban metro corridors. MIRI integrates displacement rate, cumulative displacement, local displacement heterogeneity, height-related variability, and temporal residual variability, thereby extending conventional velocity-based interpretation toward corridor-scale displacement-risk diagnosis. The framework was applied to the Kunming Metro Network using 198 descending Sentinel-1 images acquired from March 2018 to December 2024. The time-series InSAR results show that the network was generally stable, with line-of-sight (LOS) displacement rates mainly ranging from −6 to 3 mm/yr and 97.21% of coherent scatterers falling within the stable interval of −3 to 3 mm/yr. Localized displacement anomalies were mainly observed near line intersections, curved sections, construction-affected areas, and the Dianchi Lake shoreline. Based on MIRI, 78%, 17%, 4%, and 1% of valid points were classified as Levels D, C, B, and A, respectively, indicating a distribution dominated by lower diagnostic levels, with spatially confined Level-B and Level-A anomalies particularly along Lines 4 and 5 and in lakeside soft-ground areas. Multi-source contextual variables and XGBoost-SHAP analysis were further used to interpret the environmental, anthropogenic, operational, and displacement-gradient information associated with the MIRI-based diagnostic patterns. The results indicate that the spatial variation in MIRI is mainly associated with terrain-related conditions, corridor-scale displacement variation, metro operation, and urban development intensity. By deriving diagnostic levels directly from PSI-based displacement indicators, MIRI provides an InSAR-based screening tool for prioritizing displacement-related anomalies and supporting maintenance of urban rail transit systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Urban landscape patterns strongly affect air quality, yet existing studies often neglect anisotropic processes, rely on arbitrary fixed-radius buffers, and lack standardized classification frameworks. To overcome these limitations, we developed a novel analytical framework that integrates high-accuracy Local Climate Zone (LCZ) classification, multi-scale spatial modeling, and a shape-adaptive influence zone. Central to this framework is the Adaptive Directionally Weighted Sector Influence Zone (ADWSIZ), a new method that explicitly captures the directional heterogeneity of landscape effects on pollutants. Applied to station-based NO 2 measurements in the Yangtze River Delta (YRD) urban agglomeration, we used multi-scale Geographically Weighted Ridge Regression (GWRR) to identify a 4000 m circular buffer as the optimal modeling scale, revealing a clear decoupling from the intrinsic static LCZ pattern scale (approximately 1000 m). Building upon this optimal distance, the ADWSIZ model was developed to refine the influence zone’s shape. The application of ADWSIZ improved model performance (Adjusted R 2 : 0.464 → 0.506 ; RMSE: 2.950 → 2.834 µg/m 3 ) and reduced residual spatial autocorrelation. Our findings highlight that the anisotropic influence of features like industrial land and compact high-rise fabrics is a critical, previously underexplored factor in explaining NO 2 variation. This study establishes a replicable and transferable methodology that advances the conceptualization of influence zones from simple distance-based buffers to scale- and shape-adaptive forms, offering a robust tool for refining spatial coupling analyses between urban morphology and air pollutants.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Building 3D profiles constitute critical factors in urban environmental analyses, including assessments of urban heat island effects, carbon emissions, population distribution, and related challenges. LiDAR data provide comprehensive high-resolution 3D information, enabling the acquisition of detailed 3D building profiles. However, in dense urban environments, buildings are in diverse shapes and heights, and are close to one another, making it challenging to extract building instances. In this paper, we propose BuildGroup, a novel building instance segmentation model for both Airborne Laser Scanning (ALS)-based and Unmanned Aerial Vehicles (UAV) datasets. It employs the progressive learning strategy in the feature aggregation, which uses prior 2D knowledge from the Segment Anything Model (SAM) to infer spatial relationships within point clouds and mitigate cross-source discrepancies. An offset enhancement module is introduced to mitigate the effects of diverse point densities, improving instance clustering by compacting point cloud distributions. Additionally, an edge-enhanced loss function prioritizes boundary point accuracy during training to enhance morphological precision. Furthermore, we present a building LiDAR dataset with instance-level building information, which fills a key gap in real-world, large-scale urban point cloud segmentation, particularly for ALS-based sparse façade data. Experimental results validate the framework’s capability to produce accurate building instance information across varying urban densities. All code and data are available at https://github.com/littlebeen/BuildGroup .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-baseline (MB) phase unwrapping (PU) breaks through the limitations of the phase continuity assumption, but existing MBPU algorithms cannot adapt well to noisy environments. A robust reinforcement learning integer programming multi-baseline PU algorithm with JPEG-LS (RL-MBPU) is proposed. First, the cutting plane–pure integer programming cut-selection process is formulated as a sequential reinforcement-learning problem. An efficacy-guided imitation strategy is used to initialize an MBPU-structure-aware single-cut policy, which is further optimized by an actor–critic method using Cut-PAR2-based trajectory returns to reduce the root-node proof cost. Finally, the initial ambiguity number solution is corrected using dynamic median edge detector based on JPEG-LS, and the PU is completed by using the prediction of the left, top, and top-left neighbors of the current pixel and combining it with the local gradient threshold determination. In both simulation and real data experiments, the RL-MBPU algorithm demonstrated stable PU performance, including computational efficiency and noise robustness for interferograms of different sizes. Compared with the minimum-cost flow and cutting plane–pure integer programming algorithms, the RL-MBPU algorithm reduces the root mean square error by approximately 61.2% and 14.5%, respectively, while improving efficiency by approximately 54.1% and 42.4%, respectively. This can further support high-precision interferometric synthetic aperture radar applications.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Land surface temperature and emissivity Airborne Observatory System (LAOS) provides land surface temperature (LST) and spectral land surface emissivity (LSE) at very high spatial resolutions (0.5–4 m) and flexible (e.g., intraday) acquisition times. LAOS enables: (i) to map spectral variations in LSE—an inherent property of land surface materials—and (ii) to analyse spatial and temporal variations in LST in relation to land surface processes. Here, we present the scientific rationale for developing the system, the components of the hardware and the processing chain, and retrieval and validation results from an agricultural case study. The LAOS hardware consists of a HyperCam-LW (Telops Inc.), a Fourier transformed infrared spectrometer that operates in the long-wave infrared (7.7–11.5 µm) and an associated airborne platform. The processing chain includes radiometric calibration, generation of ortho -photo mosaics, noise removal, atmospheric correction (AC) and temperature and emissivity separation (TES). Key innovations with respect to previous airborne hyperspectral TIR studies are an improved noise filtering step and a recalibrated minimum emissivity—spectral contrast relationship derived from an extended set of ECOSTRESS spectral library spectra . For demonstrating LAOS’ capacities, an extensive survey campaign was conducted over the Burana Irrigation Consortium area near Modena, Italy, on 13–15 July 2022. Images were acquired at 1- and 4-meter resolution during three times of the day. Raw image tiles were processed to LST and LSE mosaics and compared with ground measurements. Validation was performed over agricultural surfaces – wheat stubble, melon, and water –providing a more surface-type-diverse assessment than previous more urban-focused studies. The LST mosaics show good agreement with the in-situ temperatures across all three surface types (within-class R 2 = 0.52–0.86, RMSE = 2.0–3.2 K, global R 2 = 0.96). LSE maps reveal crisp changes at field boundaries and appear relatively constant over time (i.e., both during a day and across multiple days). Airborne LSE spectra show mostly reasonable agreement with ground measurements and reproduce the overall spectral shape. Spectral emissivity remains largely stable both diurnally and across days (average variability ∼ 1.1 %), indicating consistent retrieval performance at field scale. The results demonstrate that LAOS produces LST and LSE maps with accuracies consistent with previous hyperspectral TIR studies, showcasing its potential to address pressing questions in various fields, including agriculture (e.g., irrigation), forestry (e.g., stress detection), defence (e.g., target detection), and urban studies (e.g., urban heat islands). As a high spatial resolution airborne TIR imager, LAOS can also serve as a demonstrator for upcoming satellite missions, such as LSTM (Land Surface Temperature Monitoring, ESA), TRISHNA (Thermal Infrared Satellite for High-Resolution Applications, CNES & ISRO), and Eagle-TIR (Explorer for Artemis Geology Lunar and Earth, NASA).

</details>

Abstract (英文): The availability of multiple long-term reconstructed solar-induced chlorophyll fluorescence (SIF) datasets at varying spatiotemporal resolutions provides new opportunities for investigating vegetation dynamics, particularly for estimating gross primary productivity (GPP) and assessing ecosystem responses to environmental stress. Evaluating the consistency and performance of reconstructed SIF (SIFr) products across different land cover types is therefore essential for their effective application in carbon–water cycle studies. In this study, we conducted a comprehensive assessment of nine widely used SIFr products (GOSIF, CSIF, RTSIF, LTSIF, HSIF, JJSIF, PKSIF, DSIF, and HCSIF) across China at a spatial resolution of 0.05° and a monthly temporal scale over the period 2007–2016. A multi-source validation framework was employed, integrating tower-based SIF, site-level and gridded GPP, and the Standardized Precipitation Evapotranspiration Index. We found that although all SIFr products exhibited strong agreement in long-term spatial patterns, substantial discrepancies existed in interannual trends. Agreement in long-term mean spatial patterns was generally higher than that in the spatial patterns of temporal trends and varied across land cover types, with stronger agreement observed in grasslands and croplands and lower agreement in forests. Across multiple validation datasets, RTSIF shows comparatively favorable performance, followed by CSIF and GOSIF, which exhibit similar performance. CSIF performs relatively well for GPP but shows lower performance in representing meteorological moisture conditions. Furthermore, product performance exhibits clear land cover dependence, with more reliable results in structurally simple ecosystems and reduced performance in complex canopy environments. We also identified differences in the relative performance of SIFr products across application scenarios and land cover types. These findings highlight the importance of product selection tailored to research objectives and environmental conditions and provide useful information for SIF-based monitoring of vegetation dynamics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Soil bidirectional reflectance distribution function (BRDF) is strongly influenced by soil physicochemical properties, yet most hyperspectral retrieval studies rely on single-view reflectance, leaving the value of multi-angle information insufficiently explored. In this study, bidirectional reflectance factors (BRFs) were measured at 48 viewing angles for 156 soil samples collected from a region dominated by Ali-Perudic Argosols. An optimal multi-angle retrieval framework was developed for different soil properties by systematically comparing spectral preprocessing methods, feature extraction, and modeling algorithms. The effects of viewing angle on retrieval accuracy and feature wavelength distribution were further analyzed, and the applicability of simulated multi-angle BRFs generated using SOILSPECT model was further assessed. Results show that the optimal framework for single-view reflectance combines Savitzky–Golay (SG) smoothing, a hybrid random frog–competitive adaptive reweighted sampling (RFROG–CARS) feature selection strategy, and nonlinear support vector machine (SVM) regression. Retrieval accuracy varied substantially with viewing geometry, and no universal optimal viewing angle was identified across soil properties. For multi-angle BRF data, a two-dimensional convolutional neural network (2D-CNN) feature extraction approach outperformed the RFROG–CARS wavelength selection method. Specifically, R 2 (RMSE) improved from 0.64 (8.8 g/kg) to 0.73 (7.7 g/kg) for SOM content, from 0.53 (6.9 %) to 0.61 (6.3 %) for sand content, and from 0.64 (4.7 %) to 0.69 (4.4 %) for silt content, while remaining comparable for cation exchange capacity (0.77/2.4 cmol/kg to 0.76/2.4 cmol/kg) and clay content (0.80/4.3 % to 0.79/4.3 %). Although the retrieval accuracy based on multi-angle BRFs remained slightly lower than that obtained from the optimal viewing geometry, it consistently outperformed most individual viewing angles. The selected multi-angle hyperspectral features were distributed across nearly all viewing angles, indicating complementary spectral–geometric information. Although retrieval accuracy based on simulated BRFs was lower than that based on measured BRFs, the results were broadly comparable. These findings support the use of multi-angle information in soil spectroscopy and remote sensing applications.

Abstract (英文): Land surface temperature (LST) is a critical parameter for characterizing surface energy balance and hydrothermal processes. While Landsat provides invaluable LST observations at medium spatial resolution for over 40 years, its native spatial resolution of thermal bands (e.g., 100 m) remains insufficient compared to its 30 m optical bands, failing to meet the demands of fine-scale studies. To address this issues, this study proposes a progressive self-training framework for downscaling Landsat LST to 30 m without relying on fine-scale ground truth, while maintaining minimal data dependence. The framework progressively optimizes a cross-modal fusion network to refine thermal details in a coarse-to-fine manner, characterized by one pre-training and two fine-tuning stages. Spatial validation against SDGSAT-1 30 m LST and temporal validation using in situ measurements confirm its reliability and accuracy, with both station-averaged MAE and RMSE outperforming the official cubic product by approximately 0.4 K. Further performance comparison experiments demonstrate that the proposed framework consistently reconstructs coherent fine-scale thermal patterns while preserving spatial heterogeneity. Multi spatial resolution evaluations and ablation studies verify the effectiveness of the proposed strategy and network design. Overall, the framework provides a stable pathway for enhancing the spatial resolution of Landsat LST, providing fine-resolution data support for fine-scale surface process studies and localized environmental monitoring.

Abstract (英文): Living in a changing climate, human society now faces more frequent and severe natural disasters than ever before. As a consequence, rapid disaster response during the "Golden 72 Hours" of search and rescue becomes a vital humanitarian necessity and community concern. However, traditional disaster damage surveys routinely fail to generalize across distinct urban morphologies and new disaster events. Effective damage mapping typically requires exhaustive and time-consuming manual data annotation. To address this issue, we introduce Smart Transfer, a novel Geospatial Artificial Intelligence (GeoAI) framework, leveraging state-of-the-art vision Foundation Models (FMs) for rapid building damage mapping with post-earthquake Very High Resolution (VHR) imagery. Specifically, we design two novel model transfer strategies: first, Pixel-wise Clustering (PC), ensuring robust prototype-level global feature alignment; second, a Distance-Penalized Triplet (DPT), integrating patch-level spatial autocorrelation patterns by assigning stronger penalties to semantically inconsistent yet spatially adjacent patches. Extensive experiments and ablations from the recent 2023 Turkiye-Syria earthquake show promising performance in multiple cross-region transfer settings, namely Leave One Domain Out (LODO) and Specific Source Domain Combination (SSDC). Moreover, Smart Transfer provides a scalable, automated GeoAI solution to accelerate building damage mapping and support rapid disaster response, offering new opportunities to enhance disaster resilience in climate-vulnerable regions and communities. The data and code are publicly available at https://github.com/ai4city-hkust/SmartTransfer.

Abstract (英文): Optical remote sensing imagery is frequently degraded by cloud and cloud-shadow contamination, which limits its reliability for near-real-time land use and land cover (LULC) mapping. Although synthetic aperture radar (SAR) can provide cloud-penetrating structural information, existing SAR–optical fusion methods often assume reliable optical observations and insufficiently address the semantic uncertainty introduced by cloud contamination. To address this issue, we propose CloudLULC-Net, an end-to-end heterogeneous SAR–optical fusion framework that directly predicts LULC maps from cloud-contaminated Sentinel-2 imagery and temporally adjacent Sentinel-1 SAR observations. The proposed network first employs optical reliability modulation to adaptively regulate unreliable optical responses, then uses heterogeneous information adaptive aggregation to progressively model cross-modal spatial and channel interactions between optical and SAR representations, and finally applies a unified semantic mapping transformer to organize the fused features in a LULC-oriented latent space. A semantic anchor-guided optimization strategy is further introduced to improve the consistency of intermediate semantic representations. To support this task, we construct CloudLULC-Set, a large-scale benchmark dataset containing 40,223 curated SAR–optical–label triplets with pixel-level LULC annotations across diverse geographic regions and cloud conditions. Experimental results show that CloudLULC-Net achieves an OA of 86.60%, an F1-score of 83.29%, and an mIoU of 73.51%, outperforming the second-best method by 0.94, 1.25, and 0.78 percentage points, respectively. Product-level reference comparisons with existing global LULC products and analyses under different cloud-cover levels further demonstrate the robustness and practical value of CloudLULC-Net for target-date LULC mapping in cloud-prone regions. The project is publicly available at: https://github.com/RSIIPAC/CloudLULC .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Spoken queries provide a natural, hands-free way to identify targets in dense remote-sensing imagery. Existing referring segmentation benchmarks in this domain accept only written expressions. We introduce VoiceAeroRef, a spoken-query benchmark derived from RISBench. It adds synthetic speech with diverse accents and voices while preserving the source images, masks, and data splits. A hard test set combines rotor, wind, and mixed interference with three signal-to-noise levels. We also propose AeroReformer2, a bilateral network for efficient speech-conditioned segmentation. The network retains speech tokens and conditions visual features at two scales through kernel linear attention. This avoids constructing a dense speech–visual affinity matrix. A detail path and a high-resolution refinement head preserve object boundaries. On the clean test split, the Swin-Base version achieves a mean intersection over union (mIoU) of 62.09% and an overall intersection over union (oIoU) of 68.22%. These scores exceed those of the strongest audio-adapted remote-sensing baseline by 5.38 and 2.08 percentage points, respectively. The model also achieves the highest mIoU on the hard test set, at 54.09%. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first study to introduce a benchmark and model for full-sentence spoken-query referring segmentation in remote-sensing imagery. The project, along with code and dataset, is available at https://lironui.github.io/AeroReformer2/ .

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sub-kilometer craters are essential for resolving fine-scale Martian surface chronology, yet their global-scale mapping remains limited by the poor generalizability of existing methods and lack of systematic global-scale validation. Here, we propose Globe-CraterSAM, a training-free and generalized framework for sub-kilometer Martian crater mapping. Central to our framework is an adaptation strategy that identifies craters from Segment Anything Model (SAM)-segmented landform units by exploiting common circular depression characteristics of craters. Before crater identification, we introduce a Buffer-Split Loop Strategy (BSLS) to separate overlapping landform units, preventing adjacent landform units from distorting the circular-depression morphology of craters. A circularity index is then applied to exclude non-circular landform units. We further reconstruct 6-m-resolution quasi-3D terrain from monocular optical images using a Shape-from-Shading-based approach and apply Double-Gaussian Fitting to define quantitative criteria for crater-like depressions. This design enables topographic information to be incorporated into sub-kilometer crater mapping despite the limited resolution of existing global Digital Elevation Models (DEMs). To address the lack of systematic validation for sub-kilometer crater mapping, we construct the first globally comprehensive benchmark dataset, comprising more than 10,000 craters from 50 sub-areas spanning 26 distinct landform types across Mars. Validation results demonstrate the strong generalizability of Globe-CraterSAM, with an average F1 score of 90.09%, outperforming nine state-of-the-art (SOTA) models by 12.38–65.88 percentage points. The framework also demonstrates strong feasibility for large-extent applications, achieving an F1 score of 91.58% across 12 1° × 1° longitude-latitude tiles. Together, Globe-CraterSAM and the benchmark dataset provide a scalable foundation for sub-kilometer crater mapping and open new opportunities for high-resolution planetary investigation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Nighttime light (NTL) serves as a key indicator of human presence on Earth. Compared with panchromatic NTL data, multiband observations provide richer spectral information for characterizing artificial illumination. However, no existing dataset simultaneously offers daily temporal resolution and multiband RGB information, limiting analyses of short-term spectral dynamics of nighttime lighting. This study proposes a multi-source data fusion-based reconstruction framework to generate daily nighttime light RGB (DNTL-RGB) data at 500-m resolution, enabling continuous characterization of short-term spectral dynamics of artificial illumination across China. The proposed framework explicitly decomposes panchromatic NTL signals into RGB components. Band-specific feature sets for the red, green, and blue channels are constructed by integrating information from NPP-VIIRS daily observations, SDGSAT-1 RGB imagery, and auxiliary geospatial variables. Independent Light Gradient Boosting Machine (LightGBM)- based estimation models are developed for each RGB band to estimate band-specific NTL intensity, enabling explicit characterization of heterogeneity among RGB components. The reconstructed results are evaluated using multi-scale validation strategies, including pixel-level and city-level comparisons with SDGSAT-1 RGB observations. The framework preserves spatial distribution patterns and achieves strong temporal consistency with SDGSAT-1 RGB observations, demonstrating its robustness in recovering RGB nighttime intensity. At the pixel scale, the reconstructed bands exhibit strong correlations with SDGSAT-1 RGB dataset ( R = 0.882, 0.895, and 0.797 for the red, green, and blue bands, respectively), while city-level agreement is further improved ( R > 0.97). High structural similarity and peak signal-to-noise ratio metrics indicate stable structural consistency and high signal fidelity across RGB channels. By enabling daily nighttime light RGB reconstruction, the proposed framework provides a scalable solution for high-frequency spectral monitoring of short-term dynamics in artificial illumination. The DNTL-RGB data serve as an empirical demonstration of the framework and support quantitative analyses of urban activity fluctuations, socioeconomic processes, human–environment interactions, and sustainable development.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Limited labeled data remains a central challenge for deep-learning-based sea-ice type mapping, while cross-channel relationships remain underexploited, and interaction mechanisms are lacking. This study proposes ESICM, combining Sentinel-1 SAR, AMSR2 brightness temperatures, and ERA5 environmental variables for pixel-level mapping of open water (OW), young ice (YI), first-year ice (FYI), and multi-year ice (MYI). ESICM adopts prototype-based metric learning to fully exploit labels, incorporates a channel multiplication-division module to enhance cross-channel interactions, and introduces two interaction functions, supports-input selection and user dense prompts, to improve classification in hard-to-classify scenes. On the AI4Arctic dataset, trained on 492 AI4Arctic scenes and tested on 20 scenes, ESICM achieved 93.34% overall accuracy, 93.08% F1 score (F1), 88.40% Kappa coefficient, and 77.29% mean intersection over union, exceeding the second-best results by 0.85%–1.55%. Under challenging melt-affected conditions, MYI F1 and intersection over union exceeded the second-best results by 3.13% and 3.93%. Across forward-temporal, cross-region, reduced-training-data, and cross-dataset experiments, ESICM ranked first for 14 and second for two of 16 metrics, indicating relatively strong generalization. In two hard-to-classify scenes, simulated user prompts covering 10% of valid pixels improved all four metrics by more than 4% and 20% over prompt-free inference, respectively, supporting the technical feasibility of interaction. SHAP attributions were broadly consistent with established sea-ice remote-sensing knowledge, enhancing the model’s post hoc interpretability. Overall, ESICM improved sea-ice mapping with limited labeled data and showed application potential under controlled distribution shifts and simulated interaction.

</details>
